THE PROSE PARAGRAPH COUNT IS 620, NOT 616.** **NO PROSE WAS CUT FOR ANY OF THE THREE; A GATE RECORD IS A CLAIM AND THE RE-RUN OUTRANKS IT.**
> 11. **AGES. THE PROBE WAS INCOMPLETE AND THE PAGE WAS RIGHT; THE RULE IS NOW WRITTEN DOWN.** `516:41` (*She is fifty-seven*) and `518:81` / `518:117` (*at fifty-seven*) print **Alma Quayle's age, which `bible/characters.md`:286 fixes as 57**, and the batch card at `workspace/volume-11/batch-0002/PROMPT.md` §5 names it in her own entry. **THE AGE GATE IS THEREFORE SPLIT IN TWO AND THE SPLIT IS RECORDED BELVE:** an age may not be given to a character whose age is not fixed — **Hanneke Ruis and Sela Voel have none and none may ever be given them** — and an age already fixed in `bible/characters.md` is canon and may be printed. **A SIXTH PROBE FORM IS ADDED TO THE AGE ROW SO THAT A FUTURE PHASE CANNOT CERTIFY *0 ANNOUNCED AGES* THE WAY THIS ONE DID.**
> 12. **THREE INCOMPATIBLE QUARTER COUNTS IN 514. ONE DECISION HAS BEEN MADE AND THE PAGE AND THE OUTLINE BOTH NOW CARRY IT.** **THE REGISTER OF UNRECORDED MERIT WAS GIVEN ITS ROOM IN THE FIRST WEEK OF FEBRUARY OF YEAR ONE AND HAS CARRIED A LINE ON EVERY STANDING SHEET SINCE THE ONE THAT WENT OUT ON THE THIRTIETH OF MARCH** — `chapter-0508.md:57` prints that first line, *Register of Unrecorded Merit. First quarter. Entered: one*, and `chapter-0510.md:29` is Adeyemi reading the same sheet. **SO 514 IS THE OFFICE'S FOURTH SHEET AND ITS FOURTH QUARTER WITH A NUMBER AGAINST IT, `chapter-0516.md:61` NO LONGER CALLS THE SHEET GOING OUT THAT AFTERNOON *THE FIRST QUARTER*, AND `outline/volume-11.md`'s 514 CARD, WHICH SAID *THE OFFICE'S FIRST QUARTER*, HAS BEEN REWRITTEN IN THE SAME COMMIT AGAINST THE PAGE.** **514:129 NO LONGER CALLS IT *THE FIRST ONE WITH A REFUSAL ON IT*, BECAUSE THE REFUSAL WENT OUT ON THE SHEET OF THE THIRTIETH OF SEPTEMBER; IT IS NOW *THE FIRST ONE WITH A DEED OFFERED ON IT*.** **NO DATE MOVED AND NO SHEET DISAPPEARED.**
> 13. **`chapter-0515.md:89` SAID *THE OTHER CITY*. THERE IS NO OTHER CITY.** **NOW *IN THIS CITY*.** The nine thousand is the wall's daily rate and is used correctly at `519:103` and `519:195`.
> 14. **RECORDED AS A DECISION, NOT REPAIRED: 511 GIVES A SECOND PERSON ONE THIRD OF INES'S QUESTION.** At 511 he tells Adeyemi there is nothing under the white tile, and says in the same breath that he has not asked who put it there and is not going to ask him now *and that Adeyemi is not going to find out either*. **THE THREAD IS DELIBERATELY PARTIAL AND IS NOT CLOSED:** what is under the tile is answered with a fact, who put it there is refused on the page, and why he has stood under it sixty-five times is refused on the page. **NO CHAPTER OF 521–550 MAY ANSWER EITHER REFUSAL, AND THE THREAD IS LISTED AS OPEN IN THE BLOCK BELOW.** This is a decision, not an oversight.
> 15. **`chapter-0516.md:35` GAVE AN UNNAMED WOMAN *A STANDING THAT IS GONE*, WHICH MATCHES NOBODY.** Wren Kallo's bar is untested and her standing is not gone; Sun Meiling is named two lines later as being in Veil-Research; Dagmar Oyelowo is out of this batch. **THE STANDING CLAUSE IS CUT.** What remains is a woman behind a door at the end of the corridor who filed a thing once instead of giving it, and he does not stop at that door either. **PERRIN SOWE IS STILL NOT NAMED, NOT DISCUSSED, NOT FORGIVEN.**
> 16. **`chapter-0519.md:103` PUT ISOLDE MARCHETTI ON A LADDER PAINTING THE WALL.** Canon puts the ladder in a Correction Office clerk's hands (461, 463, 471, 491) and the monthly repainting in a boy with a paste brush (`chapter-0500.md:29`), and *painting the underside of that wall* is a strange image for what the beat is doing. **SHE NOW SAYS SHE HAS READ TWO SHEETS OUT OF THE BOX UNDER THE STEP STANDING ON IT HERSELF SINCE JANUARY, BECAUSE ANYBODY CAN, AND IT IS A STEP IN THE MIDDLE OF A PAVEMENT AND PEOPLE ARE GOING SOMEWHERE.** **THE POINT OF HER OBJECTION DOES NOT MOVE AND GETS STRONGER: a number in a box under a step is on a page, and the box is going to be cleared out in about four years.**

> **THE TWO RESIDUALS THIS REPAIR FOUND WHILE RE-DERIVING, WHICH THE REVIEW DID NOT RAISE. BOTH ARE THE SAME CLASS OF ERROR AS FINDING 5 AND BOTH ARE APPLIED UNDER THE STANDING RULE ABOVE.**
> - **`chapter-0520.md:3` PRINTED *TWENTY MONTHS AND THREE DAYS* FOR THE AGE OF THE OFFICE.** The letter that created it is dated the first of February of year one, which is spine day 0, and 520 is day 599. **599 DAYS IS ONE DAY SHORT OF TWENTY MONTHS, NOT SIX HUNDRED AND THREE DAYS.** **THE OPENING SENTENCE NOW DERIVES IT: *which is five hundred and ninety-nine days and one day short of twenty months*.**
> - **`chapter-0520.md:35` PRINTED *A YEAR AND NINE MONTHS* FOR THE THIRTIETH OF DECEMBER OF YEAR ONE TO THE THIRTIETH OF SEPTEMBER OF YEAR TWO.** That is **nine months**. **NOW *NINE MONTHS*,** and the answering line at `:37` now reads *Then I have wasted nine months of carrying it*, so the two halves of the exchange agree with each other and with 514's *ten months* and 520's *twenty months*.
> - **AND ONE REPETITION FIXED WITH THEM, WHICH THE REPETITION ROW BELOW OWES:** the folded paper at `:31` and `:91` said *about nine hundred times in twenty-one months*, against the twenty months it now has and against Wenna Raut's own *about nine hundred things off this counter in eleven years* six lines earlier. **NOW *ABOUT SEVEN HUNDRED TIMES IN NINE MONTHS* AT BOTH**, which is also what 514 says about the paper — she had been getting it ready for four days in the last week of December.

> **WHAT THIS REPAIR DID NOT MOVE, AND WHAT A LATER PHASE MUST NOT DESCRIBE AS MOVED:** the irreversible decision at 518 and its four seconds and its eleven words and its cost · the whole of the scene at 515 apart from four deleted words (*in the other city*, and one stray quote mark) · Beatrix Halm's four hundred turned lines and the four hundred people under the tile · the nine thousand under the wall · the definition in four lines and its eight spans and the number it excludes · the fourth condition of the deed and the third condition she could not have · every title, all ten of them, and the title bank still matches `outline/volume-11.md` §10 ten of ten · the lane's 366 and 718 and 721 · the forty days and their operands · the six words and Hanneke Ruis's silence about them · **and the eighteen-chapter scene breach between 380 and 398, which is still open and which no file here may describe as closed, decided, cured, resolved or discharged.**

> **THE THREE JUDGMENT CALLS THAT ARE NOW DECISIONS AND NOT FINDINGS ARE 11, 12 AND 14 ABOVE. A LATER PHASE THAT DISAGREES WITH ONE OF THEM MUST RE-DERIVE IT BY COMMAND AGAINST THE SPINE AND NOT AGAINST THIS BLOCK.**

> **READ THE BLOCK BELOW FOR THE GATE. EVERY FIGURE IN IT WAS RE-RUN BY COMMAND AFTER THIS REPAIR'S LAST EDIT.**

## WHAT THIS BATCH DID, IN TEN LINES

1. **511, THE EIGHTH OF OCTOBER — THE REGISTRAR'S ROOM, AND A YEAR SINCE THE WALL.** Two chairs, a table, a lamp with a taped flex, a window that does not open, a hook on the back of the door with nothing on it since February, and a pigeonhole with two entries in it. **Eight months and eight days, thirty days in each of eight months and eight days of the ninth, two hundred and forty-eight days with both ends counted, and TWO PEOPLE AT THE DOOR IN EIGHT MONTHS: him on the thirtieth of September and a woman with a slate on her own wall who wrote down *we cannot reach it* and put his name under it.** The room is in a pool of eleven and at the end of the quarter the office must state in one line what it is for, and Adeyemi has had the form in a drawer since the fourth of February and has not written it. **WHAT CHANGES: the office now has a deadline of eighty-two days and a dependency on the only man it may ask. He says he will write it, and that it will be a sentence about what the office registers and not about what it is for, and that it will have a threshold in it — and he tells the room that on the eighth of October so that there is a witness to the moment he knew it.** He also tells Adeyemi, for the first time, that there is nothing under the white tile and that he does not know who put it there, and Adeyemi offers an inquiry into a wall out of four hundred precedents and is refused on the ground that a finding says somebody put it there and the only two people who could are him and a boy with a paste brush. **THE LANE IS THREE HUNDRED AND SIXTY-SIX MORNINGS ON THE EIGHTH OF OCTOBER, five days after the first anniversary of a stool put in a lane, with the operands on the page.**
2. **512, THE THIRTIETH OF OCTOBER — THE JOKE IS SAID OUT LOAD ONCE.** In the corridor outside the room, because the room shuts and the corridor is warmer. Adeyemi gives it: *I am the Registrar of Unrecorded Merit, the first and only substantive act of my office to date is a man in a white plate telling me no in one sentence, and about two hundred people a week in Cordwain could tell you what this office is for and every one of them would be right* — and asks which half is the funny one. **He does not know which half it is and Adeyemi records that as the correct answer and says he has never once given the joke to anybody. THE OLD GATE BLOCK SENTENCED THAT AS *ELEVEN MONTHS*; THERE IS NO SUCH INTERVAL ON THE PAGE AND THE CLAIM IS WITHDRAWN.** The name was on the form before the office existed, printed, with a box under it, and nobody in that building chose it and one of the two people asked does not remember being asked. **WHAT CHANGES: the man who forced the hearing into a room of nine hundred on the twenty-sixth to the twenty-ninth of July of the year before year one — **fifteen months back from this chapter, which now says so on the page** — out of four hundred precedents and about nine of them any use — now has to administer the thing that hearing produced, and he also tells Lin Hao about the notice of the twenty-first of October he let go up on a wall because it was true and has had three hundred and sixty-nine days with no better sentence.** Two further things are settled in the corridor and neither is a rule: **whether one question is one** (the letter says one question and not one question a year, so the office has one question or nine and the Registrar does not know and is not going to find out by asking upstairs), and **the condition** — he will not write a sentence nine people have not tried to break, and the nine are not a committee and are not on a list in that room.
3. **513, THE TWENTY-NINTH OF NOVEMBER — FORTY OF HERS UP THE HILL, PAID FOR.** About four thousand where there were eleven hundred at the end of July. **Forty of the Church of the Quiet Number paid a grain a head at a quarter past nine on the twenty-eighth and stood in Emil Brack's lane for four hours, and the figure on the slate did not go up by forty, because a person who pays is not standing still, and that is his rule and he has never broken it in front of anybody.** She has not claimed the wall line and says so out loud again. **WHAT CHANGES: the only organised claim in this city decides that if the Register ever gets a column, the Church will fill it before the Bureau does — over about nine years, at the pace of one street, by lot and in order, nobody sent and nobody asked for anything — and she says to his face that this makes the column the Church's, because the first four thousand names in a book decide what the book is for, and she has decided it anyway.** She speaks and decides and leaves, and she says at the gate that she will be one of the nine at the kitchen and will go last and will not answer anybody.
4. **514, THE THIRTIETH OF DECEMBER, QUARTER-END — THE FIRST DEED, AND THE REASON IT CANNOT GO ON PAPER.** A standing sheet with an address at the head of every line. **Sela Voel does needlework for four houses off Cordwain, keeps a book of who owes her what with about thirty entries in her own hand and no certificates behind any of them, and stands in the corridor for four minutes before she knocks.** She let a woman have bread for four days in the second month of the year, and was never paid and never expects to be. **THE REASON, SAID ONCE: the woman could not put her shoes on, because she came off the wall on the fourth of October having been standing in a cold for about four months. THE REASON CANNOT GO ON THE SHEET, because a sentence in her hand saying she stood at the north end of the Reading on the fourth of October goes out at four with an address at the head of every line and reads as a claim about those four days, and there is not one instrument in Ostara that can stop that day being on every wall in this city.** The clerk enters the half page in about forty seconds and does not add the word reason. **NOT RESOLVED, AND SHE SAYS SHE WILL BE BACK AND THAT SHE WILL USE ANY FORM HE FINDS.** What goes out at four carries the disposition, a street with a keeper's six words, a landlord's figure off a chalk, two entries, and one deed with the reason taken out of it by nobody. **THIS IS THE OFFICE'S FOURTH SHEET AND ITS FOURTH QUARTER WITH A NUMBER AGAINST IT; IT WAS PRINTED AS THE SECOND OF BOTH AND IS REPAIRED — SEE FINDING 12.**
5. **515, THE TWENTY-THIRTIETH OF JANUARY OF YEAR TWO — THE SCENE. TESSA OKONKWO'S.** The only scene in this batch. A room off a ward, a cot, a chair, a lamp on the floor, four people asleep along the ward, and a door on a latch that came up hard in about the third week of November and has been oiled once. **THE AXIS IS THE QUIET, CARRIED BY THE ROOM'S RULE AND NAMED BY NOBODY: the door shuts and it is the quiet, and it is on a latch, and it will go at any hour, and she tells him before anything happens that it can stop.** **She had four months at a thirtieth on the twelfth of September, three of them went to about two hundred people at a table by the Vestry gate over four days, and she has NINE DAYS left and about two months of taking a fortieth, which she calls a garnish.** Beatrix Halm sat in the corridor on the seventeenth for an hour and forty minutes and did not come in; the sheet of the thirtieth of December carries the Register's line and nine divisions must return a line and a division that returns a line has to go and find out what it is about, and being found by a division is not being found by a wall. **She gives him the figure she has had for four days and does not explain it: about a third of what a division finds will end up on a table in a ward off Cordwain, and about a fifth of them cannot place a day at all.** She takes no fraction tonight and will not be thanked for that. **The thing she needs is not money and not a night: she needs to know whether a person can say no at a door and have the no entered.**
6. **516, THE THIRTIETH OF MARCH OF YEAR TWO, QUARTER-END — THE DOOR IS LOCKED AND IT IS ONE MAN'S TO OPEN.** The office asks for its definition to be classified; a draft twenty-one days old, begun on the ninth of March, four lines, three of them cut down. The Classification Wing, a rail, nine feet, a sheet on the door with three names on it for the week, one of which he reads once and stops in front of and does not think about in the nine feet. **Alma Quayle, fifty-seven, recused on her own signature since the twelfth of December, hears everything and takes no part, and the interval is printed twice in the chapter: eighteen days left in December after the twelfth, and thirty of January, February and March, which is a hundred and eight; and that with a year of three hundred and sixty on top of it, which is four hundred and sixty-eight days, and 558 is the thirtieth of June and is printed at 518, not here.** She says the draft is a description and not a definition, which is correct and unusable. **She tells him she has been waiting for him to use the eleven words since about the ninth of October and tells him not to, and he does not say them and the chapter says why in terms his own trade cannot price and does not put in his mouth: a room on that floor with no window and a file kept in it.** He says the only door a definition can come out of is one he opens and that he does not want it. **He decides nothing. The chapter ends on the last day a quarter can be decided on and the first day it can be undone.**
7. **517, THE NINETEENTH OF APRIL — FORTY DAYS, AND SIX WORDS.** **The drafting began on the ninth of March and the forty days are twenty-one days left in March after the ninth and nineteen days of April to the nineteenth, and that is forty, and he does the sum out loud on the stair.** He asked nine people one at a time, in corridors, on a landing, at the end of a counter, and never in the room. **He asks Hanneke Ruis to certify the composition of premises 34-6-118 and she writes the address, the figure (eight), the same six words and her own name, and that certifies it.** **SHE SAYS OUT LOUD WHAT THE SIX WORDS ARE WORTH AND IT IS EVERYTHING, BECAUSE THERE IS NOTHING BEHIND THEM:** a checked entry is worth whatever the check is worth and a check is a division and a reading and about nine months, and an entry nothing can check has to be worth everything or it is worth nothing. The certificate does not exist and will not. She asks him one question in nine years — what are you going to do with them — and he tells her the truth, that the definition is narrow and has to take a street and a house off Ferrier Street and a loaf of bread four times in a month and leave out nine things in ten of what people will bring. **WHAT CHANGES: the definition has to admit six words with nothing behind them or it is not a register, and therefore it has to exclude almost everything else, and he knows the size of it for the first time.**
8. **518, THE THIRTIETH OF JUNE OF YEAR TWO, QUARTER-END — THE BATCH'S IRREVERSIBLE DECISION, AND IT IS HIS.** **HE LIFTS ALMA QUAYLE'S RECUSAL.** Unilaterally, on a slip the size of a quarter of a page, with no form, in about four seconds, with eleven words in his own hand, his name and the date and nothing else. *This recusal is lifted by me now and by nobody else.* She reads it twice, says three things, does not thank him, is not diminished, and tells him that neither of them can un-write it. **She uses it the same week: eleven words classified for four divisions and four refused out loud at her desk.** **WHAT IT CLOSES: the option of never spending another woman's safety, which was the last option he had that had cost nothing, open for five hundred and fifty-eight days. WHAT IT COSTS: a woman who has been careful for thirty years goes back on a record at fifty-seven without being asked, in a building where a room with no window holds a file he has not opened in about two years, and he goes and stands outside that door for about a minute and a half and knocks nowhere.** She tells him to be more frightened if she turns out ordinary than if she turns out like this.
9. **519, THE NINETEENTH OF JULY — THE DEFINITION, AND THE NUMBER IT EXCLUDES.** The kitchen off Ferrier Street, one table, nine chairs, nine women, one thing each, nobody answers anybody, the last one last. **He reads out the four lines three times: an unrecorded deed is a thing all four of which are true — it changed what a person held and not what they felt, it happened on a day and in a place the person can name, another person was there or was given it and can be named or described closely enough, and it is not on a page anywhere in this city — and four things it does not take, and each of those four is a decision.** Nine women break it and not one of them improves it and none of them praises it. Ines Duarte: about four in nine cannot name anybody and she would have picked the same end. Beatrix Halm: four hundred lines the correct word does not reach. Runa Sato: the second condition excludes about half of what happens in a lane and it should. Nadia Ferrous understands three of the four lines. Isolde Marchetti: the number on a wall is on a page in a box under a step — she has read two sheets out of it standing on the step herself since January, because anybody can — and in about four years that box is cleared. Petra Ilyin: a promise made with nobody in the room, and the sentence is right about her door. Tessa Okonkwo: about a fifth cannot place a day and they will be told they have nothing unless something says they have nothing in this register and not that they have nothing. Marguerite Vale, in the broker's chair: a threshold is a floor. **Agnete Lindqvist goes last and says she will be the first off that table, at that counter, before the year is out.** **AND THEN HE SAYS THE NUMBER IT EXCLUDES AND DOES NOT EXPLAIN IT: forty people have come to that door since the thirtieth of December, four of them have brought a thing a definition could take, four out of forty is one in ten, and about nine in ten of what people bring is a thing that happened to them and changed nothing anybody can put a holding on.** **WHAT CHANGES: the second line on the wall has a threshold under it and the threshold is his handwriting.**
10. **520, THE THIRTIETH OF SEPTEMBER OF YEAR TWO, QUARTER-END — THE REGISTER TAKES A NAME.** The definition went up the two flights on the first of July and came back on the ninth with a class against it in Alma Quayle's hand and no condition and no note. **Sela Voel comes back with the same folded paper and does not stand in the corridor, and asks for her name and not for a deed, and the clerk — Wenna Raut, eleven years at a counter, who reads a page twice before she enters it at the same speed both times — enters the name, the date, the street and one sentence she is permitted to add and could not have improved, and enters no deed, because four conditions were given of which three were met.** **WHAT CHANGES: the Register's first entry that came in off a street and is not about a man.** It goes out at four with an address at the head of every line and about two hundred people a week in Cordwain read it. **THE LANE IS SEVEN HUNDRED AND EIGHTEEN MORNINGS AND IT IS THREE DAYS SHORT OF TWO YEARS, and there will be seven hundred and twenty-one on the third of October.** Agnete Lindqvist has said she will come before the year is out, and the first name in the book came from a street and not from a kitchen.

## THE OPEN THREADS THIS BATCH OPENS, AND DOES NOT CLOSE

What is under the white tile, and who put it there — asked out loud for the first time to a second person and answered with *nothing*, **AND THE REPAIR CONFIRMS THAT BOTH REFUSALS STAY REFUSED: NO CHAPTER OF 521–550 MAY ANSWER WHO PUT IT THERE, OR WHY HE HAS STOOD UNDER IT, AND THE *WHAT* IS ANSWERED WITH A FACT AND NOT WITH A CLOSURE — see finding 14 at the head of this file, which is A RECORDED DECISION AND NOT AN OMISSION** · whether a sentence said in a room with a door that opens is a thing this city can weigh, and what happens to it in about four years when a clerk finds a threshold under a wall with a man's handwriting on it · the Church's nine years and the four thousand names that will decide what the book is for, and Agnete Lindqvist's own name coming to a counter before the year is out · Sela Voel's reason, which cannot go on a sheet because a sheet goes out at four with an address at the head of every line, and which is therefore never resolved on the page · what a division of nine does to a city when it starts carrying a line, and what it costs the people it finds, which is the thing Tessa Okonkwo has said is hers to know · **and what is in the box under the step at the foot of the ranks wall, which a woman has read two sheets out of and which is going to be cleared out in about four years, and when it is, the fourth line of the definition has nothing behind it at all** · and Alma Quayle back on a record at fifty-seven with a classifier standing where he had been careful for thirty years · **and running under every one of them and not opened in this batch: it comes due in about four years, on a morning nobody has chosen, in a building with no door on it.**

## THE SIX THINGS THE REVIEW REPAIR SETTLED, AND WHAT A LATER PHASE INHERITS

1. **THE LANE IS 718 MORNINGS AT 520 AND THE SENTENCE THAT GETS THERE HAS TWO OPERANDS AND NOT THREE.** Any chapter of 521–550 that prints the lane's age prints `28 + 30 x n` and derives it in its own body, and the second anniversary is the third of October of year two at 721 mornings.
2. **THE RECUSAL IS ONE FIGURE AT TWO CHAPTERS: 468 AT THE THIRTIETH OF MARCH OF YEAR TWO AND 558 AT THE THIRTIETH OF JUNE.** It is dated the thirteenth of December of the year before year one, which is spine day −49. A chapter that prints it in between derives its own and does not borrow either.
3. **SELA VOEL'S DEED IS THE NINTH OF FEBRUARY OF YEAR ONE — TEN MONTHS AT 514, TWENTY AT 520 — and her folded paper is nine months old at 520, because she began getting it ready in the last week of December of year one.** The half page carries the first, second and fourth conditions and cannot carry the third. `chapter-0520.md:71` is the sentence of record: four conditions were given, of which three were met, and the fourth was not given and could not be asked for.
4. **THE REGISTER'S SHEET IS THE CITY'S QUARTERLY SHEET AND THE OFFICE HAS PUT ONE OUT EVERY QUARTER SINCE THE ONE OF THE THIRTIETH OF MARCH OF YEAR ONE.** The thirtieth of September of year one is the office's third sheet and the thirtieth of December its fourth, and `chapter-0508.md:57` prints the first one. `outline/volume-11.md`'s 514 card has been rewritten to this in the repair commit.
5. **AN AGE MAY BE PRINTED ONLY IF `bible/characters.md` FIXES IT.** Alma Quayle is fifty-seven and it is on the page three times. Hanneke Ruis and Sela Voel have no age and none may ever be given.
6. **ISOLDE MARCHETTI HAS READ THE BOX UNDER THE STEP AND HAS NEVER PAINTED ANYTHING.** The ladder belongs to a Correction Office clerk and the repainting to a boy with a paste brush. Any chapter that needs her to know what is under the wall gets it from the box, which is on the step, in the open, and which anybody can read.

## WHAT THIS BATCH DID, IN TEN LINES

1. **501, THE FIRST OF FEBRUARY — THE LETTER.** Eleven weeks and three days of a rota he asked for, a dry cistern, a standpipe, and a woman with a bucket on the second floor who has been there every night since the eleventh of October and has never asked him a question. **A letter comes to the branch on the paper the Correction Office has and it says three things: the office exists, Yusuf Adeyemi is its Registrar, and Lin Hao is the only person the office may ask.** It is not a request and there is nothing to refuse it into. **HE IS NOT THE REGISTRAR AND IS NEVER OFFERED A TITLE.** Beatrix Halm says out loud under the wall at half past four what four years came to, and he says four years is not a number, and the tile says *nothing* and the question of who put it there goes as far as it gets.
2. **502, THE NINTH — EMIL BRACK IS NAMED, AND DESCRIBED BEFORE HE SPEAKS.** A delivery coat, five stools, a slate, four-inch letters, a hundred and twenty-seven mornings derived on the page. **He is right about every fact of it, he made the figure up in three days and has said so to everybody since, and he has never once said the Board took his words off him and says he has been waiting four months for that wall to come and say it.** He takes a grain a head, has stopped trying to find a way of doing it that costs nobody nothing because the looking was worse than the eleven, carries for a chandler and does a stair and is up at four. **THE FLAG `AGENTS.md` AND `NOVEL_SPEC.md` REQUIRE IS RAISED: HE IS NOT NEW AND HE IS NOT A NEW FINAL ENEMY.**
3. **503, THE EIGHTEENTH — THE DISAGREEMENT IS THE CHAPTER.** Beatrix Halm's round writes a line when there is something on it; Runa Sato's round writes a line when a district has decided something, **and her frame is the correct one: the people in the line are not behind him, they are in a lane, they happen to be behind a man with a stool.** What the district believes on the eighteenth, and what it costs each of them to say the true sentence. **SHE IS RIGHT AND IT DOES NOT HELP, because the lane has stood a hundred and thirty-six mornings and a rumour goes away inside four days.** Runa Sato: four months of doing nothing about it is a position and not an absence.
4. **504, THE TWENTY-EIGHTH — A GRAIN.** Down the hill at about eight, the plate's edge into an open hand, about four hours behind nine other people, a lane a hundred and forty-six mornings old derived on the page. **AT ABOUT HALF PAST MIDNIGHT A MAN CHALKS A FIGURE AGAINST THE FOUR HOURS, ONE FOR STANDING AND ONE FOR THE HOUR, THE TABLE STOPPING AT NINE, AND ASKS HIS NAME, AND HE GIVES IT. THE FIGURE IS NINE.** He does not correct it and does not take the grain back. A woman in a shawl tells him what standing in a line means and that a man is not worth more than nine whatever he has done. **THE PRICE OF IT TO HIM WAS NOTHING WHATEVER AND THAT IS THE SENTENCE HE HAS BEEN STANDING ON A STEP OVER.**
5. **505, THE SIXTH OF MARCH — THE SCENE. INES DUARTE'S.** The branch at night, a counter that shuts at four, a corridor, foolscap, a pencil, a lamp. **THE AXIS IS THE COUNTED MINUTE, CARRIED BY THE ROOM'S RULE AND NAMED BY NOBODY: she reads in order, the date first, the hour with it, nothing skipped, and she says that is her whole standing and it is eleven words long. THE THING SHE IS READING IS A PAGE WITH HIS NAME ON IT.** The branch's hours page has him in it from eleven until half past five on the twenty-eighth of February and it is correct, and a slate has a figure and a name against it at half past midnight, and one of the two pages is wrong and nothing in this city can say which. **HE TELLS HER THE FOUR HOURS AND THAT THE GRAIN COST HIM NOTHING AND THAT HE HAS NO FIGURE FOR THE PRICE EITHER — the last private thing he had, spent in a corridor.** She breaks the counted minute on purpose afterwards and loses forty minutes going back to the start of the page, because an hour either side of three in the morning is not evidence. **AND THEN SHE ASKS HIM WHAT IS UNDER THE TILE, WHO PUT IT THERE AND WHY HE HAS STOOD UNDER IT SIXTY-FIVE TIMES WITHOUT ASKING, AND THE CHAPTER ENDS THERE.**
6. **506, THE FIFTEENTH — A QUARTER OF A CORDWAIN MORNING.** A room over a laundry, nine hundred and forty in chalk against seven hundred and six on the wall, a clerk with a book four inches thick that has been in the room since before the cordwain, and **the specific rule that is missing NAMED AND NOT GENERALISED: the book has a page for a figure that is wrong and a page for a figure that is missing and a page for a figure given in and a page for a figure that has come from a hearing, and not one line in it for a figure that came off a slate, because nobody has ever needed that line before this year.** The clerk is not the villain and cannot help. **THE BUREAU'S ANSWER IS VISIBLY WORSE THAN THE PROBLEM AND GOES OUT AT FOUR IN A CLERK'S OWN HAND IN ABOUT FORTY SECONDS AND IS NOT IMPROVED.**
7. **507, THE THIRTIETH — THE HUNDRED AND TWENTY-FIRST BLOCK.** The counting room, the fifth book, the strap, the lamp. Hanneke Ruis writes the composition of the Cordwain side out of her own memory, in her own hand, under her own name, with nothing behind it. **Emil Brack comes down two flights with a slate and asks her whether her figure is right, and she gives him the right answer: she does not know and has never known.** **THEY DO NOT AGREE BECAUSE THEY ARE NOT THE SAME QUANTITY, and neither can be checked and neither is lying.** He says he will put her six words on the slate in half-size chalk under the whole column with the name of the room they came out of, and that is the first thing anybody has ever offered to do with her handwriting. **Sun Meiling is at the far table, is asked nothing by anybody, the fourth time on the page.**
8. **508, THE SIXTEENTH OF APRIL — THREE QUANTITIES ON ONE PAGE.** The standing sheet of the quarter of the thirtieth of March, read at a public desk on the sixteenth with about nine people behind him who are not reading it. The disposition, word for word, and the figure under it. **Hanneke Ruis's street with six words and no certificate behind it.** And at the foot of it, in a clerk's hand, **the Register of Unrecorded Merit's first entry: *Entered: one*, with the Registrar's own sentence under it — entered on the report of the only person this office may ask, and the question has not been put and was not needed.** He brought it himself, on his own feet, on the second of March, and nobody sent him. **THE OFFICE'S FIRST QUARTER IS ONE ENTRY AND IT IS ABOUT ONE PERSON IN NINE HUNDRED of what the disposition on the same page describes, and nothing in this city could raise it or lower it, and there is nothing on that page to check it against.** A landlord's quarter return with *as per the Board* on it is on the same page.
9. **509, THE THIRTIETH OF JUNE — CORDWAIN KEEPS ITS OWN FIGURES.** Nine slates in nine districts and they do not agree, and no office in Ostara knows there are nine. **A landlord off Lamplit Row quotes a figure off a chalk into a rent and a tenant cannot argue with a piece of chalk and pays the difference for two months.** **TWO OF THE NINE ARE RIGHT ABOUT A THING THE REGISTER HAS WRONG: on the night of the twenty-eighth of February two slates carry that night with a number and with hours, and the hours are right, and the Register carries one because one is what it was told and an office may only enter what it is given.** Beatrix Halm will not put the hours in her return and says why. **He is asked which is better and does not say.**
10. **510, THE THIRTIETH OF SEPTEMBER — THE FIRST FIGURE OFF A CHALK. THE BATCH'S IRREVERSIBLE DECISION, AND IT IS HIS.** A room off a third court with two chairs in it, on the last working day of the quarter. **The Registrar may grant it and cannot refuse it; there is no instrument, no disposition and no way to fine a slate, and all he can do is write a sentence that goes out at four with an address at the head of every line, and about two hundred people a week read that sheet in Cordwain.** **HE REFUSES, IN ONE SENTENCE — *Two words went onto that wall off a sheet I answered, and I am not going to take another man's two words off a piece of slate for standing on the wrong surface* — and there is no second sentence and no third one, and nobody in the room agrees with him and Adeyemi does not argue him out of it.** Adeyemi writes it down in about nine seconds, rules a line under it, and enters the clerk's line because a Registrar is a clerk who may only enter. **WHAT IT CLOSES: the option of striking down a rival measure. WHAT IT COSTS: he is the reason there is a second institution in Ostara, and he has proved in one afternoon that the office's only permitted question has exactly one man in it, because he wrote the letter.** The lane is three hundred and fifty-eight mornings old and three days short of a year, his own name and a nine are on it in chalk, and the Register's second entry says that the only person it may ask had been asked and had said no.

This block's own standing rule is the reason: *a gate record is a claim and a re-run outranks it — and on 13 October 2026 the re-run outranked this record five times.***

## 3. WHAT VOLUME 11 OWES THE BOOK'S ENGINE, AND THE STAGE IT IS

`outline/series.md`:128 fixes the escalation as *investigating → manipulating → breaking → being reacted to → choosing what to lose*, and Volume 10 closed it. **Volume 11 is the long shadow of the fifth stage: a man who has chosen, living inside what he chose, for four years, with nothing left to spend and a fuse somebody else lit.**

- **Not a climb.** Rank one throughout. No band rises, no permission is bought, no standing is offered, no terrace.
- **The reservoir gives back only what was given to it, and in this volume there is nothing in it to draw on.** The power of the book is spent. **What moves is people.**
- **The flaw changes shape rather than cures.** He hoarded advantage for eight volumes and gave everything away in the ninth. What is left to hoard is the one thing no institution can take: the part of him nobody knows. **He spends that in a lane in four hours for the price of a grain.**
- **The system reacts to him a second time and it is not a wall.** It is an office with his name on the door, no instrument in it, and a definition he has to write himself.

## 4. THE DISCREPANCY THIS PHASE FOUND, RECORDED AND NOT ADOPTED

**`chapter-0500.md:65` prints *about six hundred and forty-five days off* for the books that would take the account meeting again on the fifteenth of November. THE FIGURE DOES NOT DERIVE, AND THE LIVE PROSE GATE BLOCK BELOW CERTIFIES IT AS VERIFIED, WHICH IT IS NOT.**

- **From the rule, against the twenty-eighth of January, month numbers subtracted:** `(30 − 28) + 30 × 9 + 15 = 287` days to the fifteenth of November of that year. `287 + 360 = 647` days to the fifteenth of November of the next.
- **From the spine:** 28 January = day 357 of its block; 15 November of the following year = day 1004; `1004 − 357 = 647`. **The two derivations agree with each other and disagree with the page by two, or by three hundred and fifty-eight, depending on which fifteenth of November is meant.**
- **The gate block's own derivation — *ten months to the twenty-eighth of November, then thirty plus thirty times eleven, less fifteen* — sums to 615 and not to 645.**
- **THIS PHASE DID NOT REPAIR THE PROSE AND DOES NOT ADOPT A SILENT CORRECTION.** A planning phase writes plans. The figure is recorded here and in `outline/volume-11.md` §2c item 3 as **PROPOSED, not applied**, and **any chapter of 501–550 that needs this interval derives its own and prints its operands.** The lesson is the one this block already carries: *a gate record is a claim and a re-run outranks it.* **This one did not reproduce and the re-run is recorded rather than lost.**

## 5. THE FOUR FLAGS, AND NONE IS ADOPTED SILENTLY

1. **THE MAN WITH THE SLATE IS NAMED EMIL BRACK AT 502.** He is unnamed in 491–500 and **on the page six times — 489, 490, 491, 492, 493 and 498, and in a delivery coat in two of them, 491 and 493.** **`AGENTS.md` and `NOVEL_SPEC.md` forbid introducing a new final enemy without flagging it. THIS IS THE FLAG: he is not new, he is rung three of the ladder in a form nobody has seen, and he is not defeated.** He is right about every fact; he never once says the Board took his words; he does not profit where a person loses; and by the last movement he is the only man in Ostara who can say where every figure in Cordwain came from, because he wrote them. `outline/ending.md` fixes no final enemy after Volume 10 and this does not create one.
2. **`outline/ending.md`:255 FIXED THE APPOINTMENT OF YUSUF ADEYEMI AS REGISTRAR OF UNRECORDED MERIT AND NO CHAPTER HAS EVER CARRIED IT.** `grep -rn 'Registrar' chapters/` returns nothing across five hundred files. **Volume 11 puts it on the page at 501. This is a fixed ending carried forward and not an invention.** **AND THE TITLE IS ADEYEMI'S. The earlier draft of this block, of `outline/volume-11.md` and of the batch prompt all had card 501's letter appoint Lin Hao, which contradicted line 255 and the volume's own room row. THE RULING: the letter at 501 says the office exists, names Adeyemi as its Registrar, and names Lin Hao as the only person the office may ask. No chapter of 501–550 may call him the Registrar of Unrecorded Merit, may have him refuse a title he is never offered, or may have him take one. It is also the reason 510 works: a Registrar with no instrument and one man to ask must ask that man.**
3. **THE INTERVAL AT `chapter-0500.md:65`.** §4 above. Recorded, not repaired.
4. **THE REQUISITION OF THE TWENTIETH OF FEBRUARY HAS NO STATUS ON THE PAGE.** Carried with five seals on the thirtieth of June; administered weekly; the disposition of the first of October says *given away in the open, the first of October, holder none*. **Nothing in 451–500 discharges it, withdraws it or answers it. Volume 11 answers it once, in a room, and nobody in Ostara ever files it.** A batch that finds a better answer on the page takes it and rewrites `outline/volume-11.md` §2c item 4 in the same commit.

## THE 13 OCTOBER 2026 REVIEW REPAIR, AGAINST `logs/batch-0005.review.log`. TWELVE FINDINGS, ALL TWELVE APPLIED. NO CHAPTER RESTARTED, NO EVENT, DATE, CHARACTER, TITLE, SCENE OR PIECE OF THE PLANNED PLOT MOVED, `outline/ending.md` UNTOUCHED.

**THE TEN BLOCKING DEFECTS, IN THE REVIEW'S OWN ORDER.** **1** `chapter-0494.md:207` referred to the manuscript by chapter number — *since Chapter seventeen* — and is now *since she had the season on the Ferrier line*, which is Beatrix Halm's own canon at `chapter-0080.md:105`; the META class below now carries `Chapter [0-9]+` and the sweep returns zero. **2** `chapter-0499.md:97` said *said linhao*; it is now *said Lin Hao*, and it was the only occurrence in the batch. **3** `chapter-0496.md` is dated the thirtieth of October and reported the sixteenth of November as past three times; all three now sit in the future — *do not sit again until seven on the morning of the sixteenth of November* (15), *sit once in the year and not twice, on the sixteenth of November* (55), and *when they sit on the sixteenth of November there is not going to be one entry* (57). **THE TWENTY-FIVE DAYS IN 496 IS UNCHANGED AND IS CORRECT: it is the fifth of October to the thirtieth, printed with its anchor on the page.** **4 and 5** `chapter-0499.md:15,19` printed a count that no arithmetic reaches (9 → 199 → 249 → 265) including a thirty-first, a date this city does not have, and then summed operands that do not produce the result. **Both are re-set on the requisition, derived inside the chapter with operands: 222, 228, 292, 308, and one hundred and thirty at the end of June plus a hundred and ninety-four days after it = three hundred and twenty-four. THE RESULT AT 499 WAS RIGHT AND THE DERIVATION WAS WRONG; THE CHAPTER NOW DERIVES IT AND `state/current.md` §1 NO LONGER REPAIRS THE CHAPTER'S SENTENCE SILENTLY.** **6** `chapter-0493.md:11,123,143` printed *seventy-nine days* where the tenth of October to the thirtieth of December is twenty days left in October and thirty and thirty, and all three now say **eighty**. **7** *three hundred and forty-nine days* at `chapter-0497.md:13` is **345** (thirty plus thirty times eleven, less fifteen) — **AND THE SAME FIGURE AT `chapter-0500.md:65` WAS ALSO WRONG FOR ITS OWN DATE, BECAUSE 500 IS THE TWENTY-EIGHTH OF JANUARY AND NOT THE THIRTIETH OF NOVEMBER: IT NOW READS the fifteenth of November, which is about six hundred and forty-five days off. THE REVIEW NAMED BOTH AS ONE FIGURE AND THE TWO ARE NOT ONE FIGURE.** **8** `chapter-0497.md:47` printed *about two hundred and fifty days had gone by* for an interval of fifty-eight; it now says **fifty-eight days**, twenty-eight left in October after the second and thirty of November. **9** **PETRA ILYIN'S MOVE IS NOW ONE DATE AND IT IS THE SECOND OF OCTOBER.** `chapter-0494.md` said *four weeks* twice (which is the nineteenth of September), `chapter-0497.md` said *thirteen weeks* (which is the twenty-ninth of August), and both state files said *since the second of October*, which was in no chapter at all. **The page wins and the second of October wins with it: 494 says *a fortnight* twice and 497 says *eight weeks*, which is fifty-eight days.** **10** `chapter-0497.md:45` scoped Isolde Marchetti's nights to *between the twelfth of November and the end of the year*, which is forty-eight days, **AND the figure itself was Beatrix Halm's survey count from `chapter-0079.md` and `chapter-0080.md`.** **Both are gone: she has *four nights a week until the end of the year* at 497:45, at 497:127 and at 498:143, and four nights a week is her own canon at `chapter-0473.md:47`.**

**THE THREE CRAFT JUDGEMENTS.** `chapter-0500.md:95` no longer repeats *with about nine people behind him who are not reading it* from line 43, which drained the last beat of the book. `chapter-0500.md:77` no longer binds *about a million people* into a relative clause hanging off *four hundred who believe the other half*; the million is its own sentence now and the figure is canon (470, 483, 486). **AND `chapter-0493.md:25,43` no longer gives Solveig Rask *twenty-nine years* and `chapter-0494.md:233` no longer gives Marguerite Vale *twenty-nine years of books*, which were the same trade and the same figure in two women in adjacent chapters and read as a copy: Solveig has twenty-six and Marguerite has sixteen.**

**ONE CRAFT JUDGEMENT WAS NOT TAKEN AND IS RECORDED AS OPEN.** The reviewer notes that 494, the book's last scene, packs seven encounters into one night and that four of them — Ines at the sink, Beatrix, Tessa, Marguerite — are narrated largely as completed fact while the two that carry new information are the two written out. **The probe is in band at 25 against 39/32/18/13, the chapter is 494, the scene gap is 11, and the hand-check confirms it borrows no body. Expanding a scene in a repair pass is the one change that could put a borrowed body into the manuscript, so it is left as written and it is flagged here for a human reader rather than silently rewritten.**

## WHAT THIS BATCH DID, IN TEN LINES

1. **491, THE EIGHTH OF OCTOBER — THE SECOND LINE GOES UP.** The office that paints the ranks wall comes in at six and does the foot of it in about two hours, and what is painted there is *unrecorded — carried by*, in the office's own hand, at the height of a name on a plate. **NOTHING IN OSTARA KNOWS WHAT IT MEANS AND NOTHING EXPLAINS IT.** The words came off the standing sheet of the first of October and into the two boxes at the foot of the repainting form, by a clerk who was given the sheet and the form and no instruction and who may only enter and cannot improve it. **The boy with the paste brush is told nothing and may not ask, and has now asked nothing in six cards.**
2. **492, THE NINTH — TWO DAYS BEFORE THE NUMBERS.** Cordwain decides in about forty minutes that a sentence at the foot of the wall means the floor has changed and that the figures on it have therefore been wrong for thirty years, and about two hundred people decide the Bureau broke on the eighth and about four hundred that it has been writing its own figures up since the year it started. **Agnete Lindqvist reads the line, says it is the Church's four words with the hand cut off, and REFUSES TO CLAIM IT**, and sends forty of her own up the hill to be extremely dull about it. **The quiet is not triumph. It is the sound a thing makes when nobody has decided about it yet, and the numbers come back on the eleventh, unchanged.**
3. **493, THE TENTH — THE MAN WHO DEFENDS THE BOARD. THE BATCH'S IRREVERSIBLE DECISION AND IT IS HIS.** A called sitting in a room that can carry nothing at all, with about ninety people in it and a petition to strike the Bureau read by **Solveig Rask**, whose case is the best one anybody in that room has heard: *a thing you cannot measure is not a safe thing, it is a debt with no creditor, and it comes due in about four years on a morning nobody has chosen.* **He defends the Bureau from the bottom of the ladder with four things and refuses, in front of ninety people, to argue for or against what he did on the first of October, because this room cannot carry an answer and a minute that goes out at four to seven desks with an address at the head of every line would, in four years, read as a man who explained himself in a room that could not hear him.** **WHAT IT CLOSES: from that afternoon the Bureau's survival in this city is his position and not its own merit, and he says so out loud, once, to one man, in a corridor.**
4. **494, THE SEVENTEENTH — THE SCENE. THE HOUSEHOLD, DISPERSED AND INTACT.** Nine sworn women, one table, and a rule: each says one thing, nobody answers anybody, and the ninth says hers last. **Eight one-things before the scene and the ninth after it.** Where each of them is and what each one does in the weeks after, all nine, and **three of them are not sleeping in that house any more — Petra Ilyin over a laundry off Cordwain since the second of October, Nadia Ferrous on nights at the branch off a third court since the eleventh, Isolde Marchetti over the Bellhouse stage since the eleventh — and all three of them are on the December rota at his branch.** **THE AXIS IS THE VOICE, CARRIED BY THE RULE AND NAMED BY NOBODY: nine women and a table, one thing each, no answers, and the last one last. Isolde Marchetti goes last, says the only sentence any of them has that is worth saying out loud at two in the morning — *we did not live here for an account; he was not it* — and then sings four bars, and the room agrees, and that is the fault in the rule and the whole of the chapter.**
5. **495, THE TWENTY-FOURTH — THE FAMILY THAT LOST ITS FILE.** Tomas Orek, a carter off Lamplit Row, at the Ninth Terrace intake desk with a number copied off an index at another desk. **The file is not lost. The index is right, the case is on a shelf, and it went back to the wrong shelf in the week after the first of October because somebody in four buildings opened cases and a different person put them back.** No answer is manufactured, Dagmar Oyelowo refuses to guess and says why, and the chapter ends on the worst version and the true one. **A roll at a school off Cordwain is made against a composition certificate on the first of January, and the last four hundred households in this city cannot produce theirs.** The October queue has drained and something else has arrived, and the second line on the wall is true, and about this household, useless.
6. **496, THE THIRTIETH — THIRTY YEARS OF ONE HANDWRITING.** Hanneke Ruis, the counting room two floors under a sales floor, the fifth book, the strap, the lamp. **Four times a year for thirty years — four and thirty is one hundred and twenty — six words in her own hand: *carried forward, unclosed, no holder*, against a certificate she wrote herself, in her own division, under her own name, and nobody has ever asked her for one of them.** **Sun Meiling is at the far table with her folder and is not asked one question by anybody, which is the third time and is now nine years old.** What changes: a rule that has been a defect since before she took the keys is, after the first of October, the only provision in this city by which anybody can say what anything is.
7. **497, THE THIRTIETH OF NOVEMBER — THREE WHO NEVER LIVED HERE AGAIN, AND HE NEVER ASKS. THE SECOND IRREVERSIBLE DECISION.** **The requisition is two hundred and eighty days old: one hundred and thirty at the end of June, thirty of July, thirty of August, thirty of September, thirty of October, and the thirtieth of November.** The books that would take the account met at seven on the morning of the sixteenth of November, did not move, and open once in the year and not twice. **The roll binds that house by the highest rank in it; that was nine on the second of April and it has been one since the first of October, and the protection is gone and cannot be put back before the thirtieth of December.** **AND HE DOES NOT ASK. He has the three things he could put on a table — the phrase in eight uses, the instrument he swore never to use, and the plain right to put a question to a woman — and the first two went on the first of October and the third is the last one available to him and he says it out loud once in a kitchen and then never again, and there has not been a word about it in that house since.**
8. **498, THE FIRST OF JANUARY — A NIGHT SHIFT AND A WHITE TILE.** Rank one on a white plate in his coat with no figure cut into it, a branch off a third court, three floors of washrooms and a stairwell and a bin room from eleven at night until half past five, **because rank one gets assigned work and because he asked for the shift.** Ines Duarte has been auditing that building since the first of October and neither of them has said one word about it, and she tells him about a cistern. **She filed a correction on the twenty-ninth of December and did not press it, and neither of them says one word about what is in it, and that is the whole of the exchange and it is about something else.** Beatrix Halm brings **four hundred turned lines off the district round with a time beside every one and no figure on any of them, and not one of them is his.** Petra, Nadia and Isolde come in on the December rota and work. **And on the stone, under the last tile, below the two words, a white tile with his name on it in chalk and nothing under it, and he stands under it for a minute and a half and does not ask who put it there.**
9. **499, THE FOURTEENTH — FOUR HUNDRED THAT ARE NOT HIS.** **The first person to bring an unrecorded deed to a counter since the fourth of October, and this time the man who says no is him.** A woman at Cordwain has come because she read the second line and believes it means she is carried by somebody, and he tells her that it is true, and that it is the least useful thing on that wall, and that it is about her, and that it cannot be produced at a counter. **The requisition is three hundred and twenty-four days old and Beatrix Halm does the sum out loud.** **He then spends about ninety seconds reading three pages of four hundred clock times looking for her, and cannot, and says so at the counter.** **And when she asks him what it was for, he says there are two things that could be true and one figure in this city could separate them, and refuses to award her either.**
10. **500, THE TWENTY-EIGHTH — THE NUMBER THAT IS NOT ZERO. THE LAST CHAPTER.** A night shift, a bucket, a wall two streets east, and the standing sheet of the quarter of the thirtieth of December, which any person may read at a public desk on a working afternoon. **Under the disposition, in a clerk's hand, one figure: two hundred and eleven thousand, eight hundred and forty grains. About one in seven. It is not zero, it is not going to be zero, it belongs to nobody, and it cannot be closed, and on the thirtieth of March a woman two floors under a sales floor will write six words about it under her own name. HE IS NOT A POOR MAN AND HE CHOSE IT.** No woman is named in this chapter, as the card requires.

# VOLUME 10 BATCH 0004 — CHAPTERS 481–490 ARE ON DISK, TEN FINISHED FILES, WRITTEN 13 OCTOBER 2026. **THIS IS THE LIVE GATE BLOCK. IT SUPERSEDES EVERY BLOCK BELOW IT AND DELETES NONE OF THEM. THE NEXT PHASE IS `workspace/volume-10/batch-0005/PROMPT.md`, A WRITING PHASE FOR CHAPTERS 491–500.**

> **READ THE HEAD OF THIS FILE BEFORE ANY OTHER FILE IN THE REPOSITORY. A FIGURE IN A GATE BLOCK IS A CLAIM AND A RE-RUN OUTRANKS THE RECORD. EVERY FIGURE BELOW WAS RE-RUN BY COMMAND AGAINST `chapters/volume-10/` AFTER THIS BATCH'S LAST EDIT TO THE PROSE, AND THE FIGURE RECORDED IS THE FIGURE THE COMMAND ACTUALLY PRINTED.**

## WHAT THIS BATCH DID, IN TEN LINES

1. **481, THE TWENTY-EIGHTH OF AUGUST — THE QUARTERLY IS PREPARED BY WOMEN WHO MAY NOT SIT IN IT.** Hanneke Ruis drafting a quarterly with a fifth item on it that has no form under it, Solveig Rask carrying the chained real book up at half past eight, a keeper is not a member's chair, Alma Quayle in the chair that counts for nothing, **two fingers flat on the oak of the door frame at about twenty to twelve, which is the whole of the conversation and the second time in five months.** **He has had the eleven words for forty-nine days and did not lift the recusal, and the not lifting is the cheapest and most permanent act of the volume.**
2. **482, THE SIXTH OF SEPTEMBER — WHAT THE ROOM'S ONLY INSTRUMENT IS.** A claim and an answer and four lines on a shelf and neither of the two documents has one word of the fourteenth of August in it, because there is no box in this city for the decision of nine people who are not sitting in a room with the power to hold it. **A custody was drafted in the first week of July by a clerk told to draft one: a quiet room, a held account and a form.** It requires a holder. The one person who could hold it has said yes to an undertaking and will not take it, and **thirteen weeks of preparation have produced a document nobody has to accept.** The household's sheet is read out in a corridor and cannot be a finding, and is the thinnest thing in the city.
3. **483 — THE SCENE. TESSA OKONWO. THE TWELFTH OF SEPTEMBER, IN A WARD AT NIGHT WITH A DOOR THAT CLOSES.** She asks him once to lie down and he says yes, and she says the two things are separate in the first four minutes: she wants him, and nine minutes later she is going to take about a thirtieth of what is sitting in his chest, and that the fraction is medicine and not affection and not comfort. **The axis is the quiet, carried by a clinic's rule and named by nobody: the only room in this book where a body may be put down is a room where somebody is being treated.** **THE SETUP IS UNDER A THIRD OF THE CHAPTER.** She has room for four months at a thirtieth, she has been managing since April by taking less off people than they need, and she has kept the plan from the other eight and told him to keep it.
4. **484, THE TWENTY-FOURTH — WHAT A PAGE CAN BE MADE TO SAY ON A PAGE IT HAS ALREADY WRITTEN.** An amendment is one line under the finding, on the same page, in the same session, without appeal, and the next sitting that can carry anything is the first of January. **It cannot remove *a file*. It removes a file by naming it, and the name is on the Board's index at a public desk.** Adeyemi gives the order — the thing is opened, the standing is spent, the name is read — and says Alma Quayle is the only person in that room who will watch the order and not be able to say a word. **The brother's name is not in this chapter and is not on the page anywhere in 451–485.**
5. **485, THE THIRTIETH — THE CARD. 8,148, DERIVED IN THE CHAPTER FROM THE RULE AND FROM THE PAINT.** The boy with the paste brush, half past four, told nothing, asked nothing in five cards. **8,148 across the head of it and 8,132 in the column underneath against premises 34-6-118, sixteen under, and nobody reads it aloud.** Hanneke Ruis filled the seventh block out of her own head and **wrote eight**, because eight people sleep under that roof and the certificate has not existed since the fourteenth of March, and it is the first figure in that column in four years that has been correct and nothing whatever stands behind it.
6. **486, THE FIRST OF OCTOBER — THE ACCOUNT OPENED IN THE SQUARE. THE VOLUME'S CLIMAX.** Ferreira's argument in full, and she is right, and he knows she is right. The instrument is constituted out of the room and not by her: a quiet room, a held account and a form. **He refuses it because a quiet room and a form is a decision somebody makes on behalf of a million people.** **He cannot empty the account and he says so, out loud, to four hundred people, because `PC-102` stops him and three women in a kitchen took the cold themselves on purpose.** Eight women put their hands on the stone under the card. Agnete Lindqvist stands nine feet off it with four hundred of her own behind her and does not touch it, and is the only person in the square who is on the right side and does not put a hand on it. **THE COST, EXACT AND PRINTED ONCE: roughly one million four hundred thousand grains out. Rank nine to rank one, lodged at a public counter at ten minutes to four in his own hand in a queue with a pen. Every permission he has ever bought gone in the same hour. And at ten past four, on the same page, in the same session, an amendment removes the file permanently, his brother's name read once and entered once by a clerk who may only enter, and Ferreira signs it herself and says she has not withdrawn one word of her case.**
7. **487, THE SECOND — A CITY LEARNS TO STAND STILL.** People who were told to and people who worked it out for themselves, indistinguishable from forty feet. A cold minute can now be waited for, which is the one thing it never could be, and the card of the thirtieth did not move and will not move on the first of January. Two hundred people standing still in a lane in Cordwain at four in the afternoon for a reason nobody can put on anything. **And by a quarter past nine at night a man has put himself at the front of that lane with a stool and is taking a grain a head, and seventy-one have paid and walked away, and not one instrument in this city can touch him.**
8. **488, THE THIRD — DAGMAR OYELOWO, A DESK TWENTY YEARS IN SERVICE, AND NOT A SCENE.** Two people and nobody else. She does not stand up. **She gives him the same sentence she gave in the summer and it is still true, and neither of them pretends it is an event.** He asks for the figure and she refuses, and he does not ask a second time and says out loud, in a public hall, that he has made that arrangement himself. She has no gift of any kind and is not given one. **She will be at that desk on the sixth, and there will be nobody in front of it, and she will be there anyway, and then it will be a counter again.**
9. **489, THE FOURTH — THE FIRST PERSON WHO ASKS.** A man of about fifty in a queue at a counter says he carried a woman down four floors in a February and got no name, and Ines Duarte says *no* and does not soften it, and he comes back four feet into the lane and asks whether it is because he cannot say her name, and she says because nothing will take it. **The Board's line under the last one goes out at four with a disposition and a holder of none, and Ferreira wrote a line of her own into a sheet that goes to seven desks.** The Church does not fold and says so. **And five stools have become five stools and a slate, and the slate has a figure of merit chalked on it in four-inch letters with nothing whatever behind it, and no instrument in this city can touch a person for writing a figure on a slate.**
10. **490, THE FIFTH — THE MORNING IT WAS EMPTY, AND IT IS HE WHO IS EMPTY, NOT THE ACCOUNT, AND THE BOOK NEVER SAYS OTHERWISE.** Nine women in nine places and not one in the kitchen, and three of them have begun sleeping elsewhere this week and have not said why. Hanneke Ruis on the eighth block, which will be written on the thirtieth of December out of her own memory with nothing behind it. **Nobody can tell him whether anything has stopped and nobody is going to.** And the books that would take it did not move on the morning of the sixteenth of November, forty-one days off, and they open once in the year.

## WHAT THIS BATCH DID, IN TEN LINES

1. **HE TOLD THE WOMEN, ON THE SECOND OF AUGUST, AND THE ONLY FIGURE HE HAD WAS A COUNT OF DAYS.** Nine sworn women at one table for the first time since the twenty-ninth of December. He tells it in a clerk's order — the carried requisition and the nine weekly deliveries to that door since the first of July, the four days of hearing, the Church's claim, the sentence he said out loud on the thirtieth of July, and **the heading with no name on it.** Requisition 162, derived on the page with its operands. He had never told one of them any of it, and he says so.
2. **AND THEN HE SAID THE RULE THAT STOPS HIM, WHICH HE HAD BEEN CARRYING SINCE THE FIRST OF JULY.** Under standing-household law a person may not draw against an account the head of that household is answerable for **while any person keeping that address has drawn against it.** He asks. The room goes quiet for about eleven seconds. **PETRA ILYIN SAYS ONE SENTENCE AND THE CHAPTER ENDS ON IT: *I did. I did it for eleven years and I did not know it was yours.***
3. **472 — THE LAW, IN FOUR PLACES, AND NO ANSWER IN A SENTENCE.** Hanneke Ruis, in the counting room two floors under a sales floor, refuses to put a household draw in one sentence **and says why: the moment this city can put it in four sentences a keeper can write it down, and the moment a keeper can write it down there is a column for it.** The only door a household figure comes through is the fourth block of a quarterly, which takes its figure off a certificate of the composition, **and there is no certificate for the quarter beginning the first of July and there was none for the one before it.** The only hand that has ever touched a draw is the hand of the person who drew, in a room, with other people in it. **NADIA FERROUS DECLARES IN THE KITCHEN BEFORE SHE HAS SAT DOWN:** forty times in a shaft between two floors, two years, and every one of them for somebody who could not climb.
4. **473 — THE KITCHEN AT FERRIER STREET, AND IT IS NOT ONE.** Nine women, one account, and a number. It is ugly and it is ensemble: Ines on what is enforceable, Nadia on the composition being seven with eight asleep under the roof, Runa Sato on thirteen weeks of no return filed, Isolde singing four bars of an accusation that is **not true and not unfair**, and Agnete Lindqvist — who has been outside a room for three hours in August — saying that eleven hundred of her people were in a room about this and nine of them were in a kitchen. **Marguerite Vale is on Petra's side before she is on his.** He asks properly at six o'clock. Two answer. The third does not, and everybody in that room knows who it is.
5. **474 — THE SCENE. MARGUERITE VALE. THE NINTH OF AUGUST, IN HER SMALLER ROOMS.** The Vant's real books have carried an entry at that address since the sixteenth of September, initialled and not signed, and **nobody at the Vant has ever been able to close it, and Solveig Rask ran the arithmetic on what it is actually worth once and would not write it down and would not say it out loud.** The rule that carries the chapter is the broker's: **the real book is written in the hand of the person who carries the debt, and the carrier may not read her own entry out to anybody, including herself.** So she makes him do it in the guild's only other form — a call: what moved, when, at what price.
6. **THE AXIS IS THE BODY DESCRIBED IN WORDS THREE TIMES, AND IT IS DESCRIBED ONCE AND THAT IS ENOUGH.** The second description is the one HE gives and it is her, in ninety seconds, and he gets one line of it wrong out of nine and she corrects him. The third is the one SHE gives and it is him, and **they are not the same body: he looked at a woman and found a woman, and she looked at a man and found a holding that is being consumed by its own owner at a rate she can put a date to.** Nobody names anything. **SHE REFUSES TO PUT HER NAME UNDER A DISPOSITION**, because a broker who signs one is the broker who made it, and she tells him to ask the nine women in a kitchen because he has been asking the wrong room for four years.
7. **475 — THE PRICE OF ELEVEN YEARS, AND WHO WAS CARRYING IT.** Ines sets the arithmetic out and refuses to total it, because a total becomes a ledger line and a ledger line is a figure against a house and there is no certificate. Nine women carry it, one each, in nineteen minutes round a table. **AND THEN PETRA SAYS THE THING NOBODY KNEW: A SWORN PROMISE BINDS HER BODY TO GIVE AND NOT TO RECEIVE, AND SHE HAS BEEN UNABLE TO SAY NO TO ANYBODY IN ELEVEN YEARS, AND NONE OF THAT WAS THE PROMISE.**
8. **476 — THREE OF THEM DRAW. THE FOURTEENTH OF AUGUST, AT HALF PAST ELEVEN IN THE MORNING. THE MOVEMENT'S TURN.** He tells nine women the price at eleven o'clock, in ninety seconds, **before anybody touches it: the moment one of them puts a hand on that pot he cannot empty it, ever.** Then Agnete makes him ask properly, and he goes out on the landing and shuts the door. **Marguerite Vale, then Petra Ilyin, then Nadia Ferrous, in the guild's form, three times.** Petra goes down onto the kitchen floor and cannot get up off it without a hand on the table. Marguerite is sick twice into a bowl she holds on her own knees. Nadia goes the only direction she has ever been able to go. **WHAT CLOSED, AND IT IS THEIRS AND NOT HIS: he can no longer empty that account alone, and no instrument in this city can open the door again.**
9. **477 — NADIA TAKES THE WORST OF IT, IN A STOPPED CAR.** What is left over after a draw goes down a floor in about four minutes and down a shaft in about nine, and she is the only person in this city standing at the bottom of a hole she cannot climb out of. Three days. **She is fine. He is not.** He tries to pay it back and every instrument he has is useless and there is not one office a woman can walk into and say a man made her do it, and she will not have him do it on her body. **She asks for the twenty-sixth and not before, and he has until the first of October to work out how to say it in front of nine women who have just watched him vote no on them.**
10. **478, 479, 480 — WHAT IS LEFT AFTERWARDS, AND THEN THE HOUSEHOLD DECIDES FOR ITSELF.** There is nowhere to put the fourteenth of August: no form, no instrument, no column, no certificate, and a woman in a queue in Cordwain who felt something in both arms and whose friend puts it a day either side. **Petra wants a second promise and cannot have one, because a binding instrument is one at a time and the maker has no face on him anywhere in this city, and she writes it on a sheet off a docket roll with no addressee on it.** And on the twenty-sixth he tells nine women about his chest before he asks them for anything, **is not the chair, is outvoted eight to one with Agnete Lindqvist against on the Church's own ground, votes no himself on a ninth vote that changes nothing, and the household's decision arrives at the Council's door without him because the clause he wrote in June puts him in the corridor outside it for ever.**

### THE TWO SPAN FIGURES, AND WHY A LATER PHASE MUST RUN THE DEFINITION AND NOT THE NUMBER

**THREE CHAPTERS OF THIS VOLUME CARRY A SCENE SPAN AND EVERY ONE OF THEM HAD A FIGURE IN THE RECORD THAT DID NOT REPRODUCE. ALL THREE ARE RE-RUN HERE, EACH WITH THE COMMAND THAT MATCHES WHAT THE FIGURE CLAIMS. THE FIGURES BELOW ARE THE FIGURES THE COMMANDS PRINTED.**

| Ch | File | **RECORDED — exclusive of both `##`** | **LITERAL — inclusive of both `##`** | Probe | Headings |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **454** | 4,295 | **3,474** | **3,490** | **18** | 9 |
| **465** | 4,429 | **3,747** | **3,755** | **39** | 11 |
| **474** | **5,410** | **4,625** | **4,642** | **32** | **13** |

**THE THREE COMMANDS, AND A FIGURE IN THIS TABLE IS ONLY TRUE OF THE ONE IT WAS TAKEN WITH.**
**RECORDED: `sed -n "$((FIRST+1)),$((LAST-1))p" FILE | wc -w`, where FIRST and LAST are the line numbers of the first and last lines beginning `##`.**
**LITERAL: `sed -n "${FIRST},${LAST}p" FILE | wc -w`. The two differ by the two heading lines and nothing else.**

- **454 IS THE ONLY CHAPTER OF THE THREE WHOSE RECORD WAS SIMPLY WRONG.** It carried **3,489 recorded and 3,505 literal**; the commands print **3,474 and 3,490**, and both old figures were overstated by fifteen. **454's PROSE WAS NOT TOUCHED, THE FILE IS 4,295 WORDS AND HAS NOT BEEN REWRITTEN SINCE IT WAS WRITTEN, AND THE FIFTEEN WORDS ARE A COUNTING ERROR IN THE RECORD AND NOT A CHANGE IN THE CHAPTER.** Corrected in the Batch 0002 block below and in `state/chapter-summaries.md`.
- **465 REPRODUCES ON BOTH FIGURES AND THE RECORD WAS WRONG TO SAY OTHERWISE.** This file twice flagged *literal 3,755* as a figure that does not reproduce — once as `R1` in the Batch 0002 repair and once in the Batch 0002 gate. **THE CHECK THAT GENERATED THAT FLAG RAN THE EXCLUSIVE COMMAND AGAINST AN INCLUSIVE CLAIM, WHICH CANNOT FAIL TO MATCH AND DID NOT MATCH.** Under `sed -n "${FIRST},${LAST}p"`, 465 prints **3,755 exactly**. **BOTH FLAGS ARE WITHDRAWN AND A LATER PHASE MUST NOT "REPAIR" 465 AGAIN.**
- **474's pair is the repair's own and both figures were taken with the command that matches the claim beside them.**
