## WHAT THE NEXT BATCH INHERITS, IN ONE PARAGRAPH

**A man told nine women what he had been carrying for a month, and the rule he was carrying was that three of
them had already touched the thing he needs to empty, and one of them had been touching it for eleven years
under a promise she cannot remember making. The law says a draw cannot be entered anywhere in this city, so
the only way to know is for a woman to say so, and three of them said so, and on the fourteenth of August
three of them did it on purpose and in the open and took the cold minutes themselves, and that cannot be
undone for them or for him. Nadia Ferrous has been sitting at the bottom of a shaft letting all of it come
down through her floor for three days and is lighter and cannot put it anywhere, and she is fine. Petra Ilyin
wants a second promise and cannot have one and has written the first eleven years down on a sheet with no
addressee on it. And on the twenty-sixth, at a kitchen table, nine women decided what they will say about it,
eight of them with him and one of them against him on the Church's own ground, and he was the ninth voice and
voted no, and the clause he wrote in June will put him in a corridor outside the door of their decision on the
first of October, for ever. Nothing has been returned. The account is exactly as full as it was on the
eleventh of July and on the thirtieth of July and on the second of August.**

---

# VOLUME 10 BATCH 0002 — CHAPTERS 461–470 ARE ON DISK, TEN FINISHED FILES, **REPAIRED ON 13 OCTOBER 2026 AGAINST `logs/batch-0002.review.log` — FOUR FINDINGS AND THREE FURTHER DEFECTS FOUND WHILE RE-RUNNING THE GATE, ALL EIGHT FIXED IN THE PROSE.** **THIS IS THE LIVE GATE BLOCK. IT SUPERSEDES EVERY BLOCK BELOW IT AND DELETES NONE OF THEM. THE NEXT PHASE IS `workspace/volume-10/batch-0003/PROMPT.md`, A WRITING PHASE FOR CHAPTERS 471–480.**

> **READ THE HEAD OF THIS FILE BEFORE ANY OTHER FILE IN THE REPOSITORY. A FIGURE IN A GATE BLOCK IS A CLAIM AND A RE-RUN OUTRANKS THE RECORD. EVERY FIGURE BELOW WAS RE-RUN BY COMMAND AGAINST `chapters/volume-10/` **AFTER THE REPAIR PASS'S LAST EDIT TO THE PROSE**, WHICH IS LATER THAN THE BATCH'S OWN LAST EDIT, AND THE FIGURE RECORDED IS THE FIGURE THE COMMAND ACTUALLY PRINTED.**

## WHAT THIS BATCH DID, IN TEN LINES

1. **THE HEARING IS CALLED AND IT IS ADEYEMI'S INSTRUMENT AND NOT A FAVOUR.** 461, the nineteenth of July, two days after the chest: a clerk of the Correction Office sets a ladder against the ranks wall at the north end of the Reading and reads a notice to about four hundred people, and **the notice carries a number, a date and not one word**. He is six feet away on the step and a woman with a shopping bag asks him what it is about, **and he does not supply the word, in the street, in front of four hundred people.** Adeyemi's whole profession in one sentence: *the only defence against a requisition is a hearing anybody may attend.* Alma Quayle is at the rail in a square of nine hundred and reads nothing on it.
2. **THE CHURCH HAS A POSITION AND IT IS NOT A HOSTILE ONE.** 462, the twenty-first: eleven hundred out of eleven thousand, **one in ten by lot in the order of a roll**, nine of a board that has never chosen her, and **her own name on a book and not the household's.** It belongs to nobody and the correct disposition is that it be given out. There is no list, so it cannot be returned. *He got what he came for and there was nothing in his coat he could have priced her at.*
3. **FOUR DAYS, SET BY A CLERK, AND A CLERK CANNOT BE ASKED TO HURRY.** 463, the twenty-third: the twenty-sixth, the twenty-seventh, the twenty-eighth, the twenty-ninth — **twenty-nine taken from twenty-six is three, and three days between the first and the last is four days in a room** — sealed in about eleven seconds. He asks for September and is told the days are the days. **AND THE MATTER PUTS ITS ONE WORD ON A WALL AT FOUR O'CLOCK AND HE WATCHES IT HAPPEN AND DOES NOT TAKE IT DOWN**, because the only way to take a notice off that wall is to go and ask who put it up, and the answer is one floor below a room that stands at five.
4. **AGNETE'S ROOM, ITS RULE, ITS SIZE AND ITS PRICE.** 464, the twenty-fourth: nothing can be compelled in a room she stands in, **she has not been outside one in eleven years**, **she has held it at nine and has never held it at a hundred and will not find out at nine hundred in the middle of somebody's sentence.** She holds it on the second day only. And she tells him on a Friday that **in her room he will not be able to refuse either**, and that he cannot stop it.
5. **THE SCENE.** 465, the twenty-fifth, Beatrix Halm, **the roof of the last car up the Ferrier hill between eleven and one in the morning.** The city's turned lines triangulated twice, and the second one came from the desk that takes her returns, and the woman on that desk is Wren Kallo, and Wren cannot hold a thing. **He has eleven seconds' worth of an instrument and he does not spend her.** The reading goes out of order and may not be taken back. **She also tells him at the end that she felt a room four streets away go quiet twice and cannot put either one of them at a date, and refuses to invent an hour — after the repair of 13 October 2026, which took the two hours off that were two days early. Recorded span 3,747 words, exclusive of both bounding `##`; literal first-`##`-to-last-`##` 3,755, inclusive of both. Both reproduce.**
6. **THE MAN WHO FILED IT INSTEAD. THE MOVEMENT'S TURN.** 466, the twenty-sixth: Perrin Sowe puts four true sentences about himself into a public record — **Lin Wei was right, he knew, he filed instead of giving, and the file is the reason Lin Wei was removed** — and the book does not punish him. **THE BATCH'S IRREVERSIBLE DECISION IS HANDS AND IT IS HIS: eleven seconds, a brass, a clerk who may only enter and a phrase unspent since the ninth of May, and he lets Sowe pay for a true sentence and does not use one of them.** The option of buying his way out of another person's ruin is closed.
7. **TWO HOURS AND FIFTY MINUTES NOBODY CAN USE.** 467, the twenty-seventh: sixty names, a hundred and forty particulars, the city, no rhetoric. **He stops a man thanking her and Agnete asks the officer to correct him if he does it twice.** Beatrix is at the back of the room with a notebook in order for the first time in four years.
8. **PUT IN THE RECORD AND KEPT.** 468, the twenty-eighth: eleven hundred and forty in a room that holds nine hundred, **the room does not hold at that size and she gives the afternoon up and says so on the record** — so the afternoon is the first of the four on which anything said can be used. Eleven wrong answers, and he corrects none of them, because a subject does not instruct the room. **Four lines and an hour and the number of a finding, held by no person, in a book on a shelf that anybody may ask to read.** Nine thousand people under a wall four streets away.
9. **THE CLAIM THE CHURCH MAKES.** 469, the twenty-ninth: four sentences, three hundred and ninety words, **built out of the second day's two hours and fifty minutes**, and the room went with her at about half past two. The disposition is not the hearing's to decide; the answer is entered for the thirtieth, in the ordinary way, and is not a fifth day.
10. **THE ARGUMENT AGAINST GOOD PEOPLE.** 470, the thirtieth: **he refuses the room she offers him, because a free statement binds nobody and he came to be held to something.** He argues out of nine days at a wall, from the eleventh to the nineteenth, and about forty of two thousand who noticed. He cannot finish one sentence three times. **He says out loud, in four hundred people, that it has never been his — and the clerk enters a heading that does not carry his name at all.**

## THE 13 OCTOBER 2026 REVIEW REPAIR OF BATCH 0002 — FOUR FINDINGS, THREE FURTHER DEFECTS, ONE BAD FIGURE IN THIS BLOCK. **NO CHAPTER WAS RESTARTED, NO PLANNED PLOT WAS CHANGED, NO RULE OF THE WORLD WAS OVERTURNED, NO PC WAS ADOPTED, WITHDRAWN OR RENUMBERED, NO TITLE CHANGED, NO SCENE MOVED, NO DATE IN THE FICTION MOVED, NO CHARACTER AGED AND NO MARK MOVED.**

**THE REVIEW IS `logs/batch-0002.review.log`. IT FOUND FOUR DEFECTS AND SAID STRUCTURALLY THAT THE BATCH IS STRONG; THE REPAIR FIXED ALL FOUR AND FOUND THREE MORE OF THE SAME CLASS WHILE RE-RUNNING THE GATE, AND ONE OF THOSE IS A FIGURE IN THIS BLOCK THAT DOES NOT REPRODUCE.**

**THE REPAIR ORDER WAS THE REVIEWER'S AND IT WAS RIGHT: F3 AND F1 ARE THE TWO A READER TRIPS OVER.**

1. **F3 — THE WALL DAYS CONTRADICTED THEMSELVES INSIDE THE BATCH, AND THIS WAS THE LOAD-BEARING ONE.** `461:3` and `461:129` fix the run: **the eleventh to the nineteenth of July, the nineteenth being the ninth day, with a queue on the eighteenth and on the seventeenth and none on the eleventh, about a hundred and ninety hands on the nineteenth, and a wall he has been sitting under for nine days.** That is the batch's own arithmetic, printed twice, and 461's opening scene cannot be moved off it — he is on the step at half past six and gets up off it and sits back down at about half past eleven. Against that, `460:13`, `460:15` and `460:125` said five days, `465:21` said eight, `468:85` and `468:87` said **since the seventeenth / not been there for eleven days**, `469:45` said eight, and `470:23`, `470:51` and `470:85` said eight with `470:53` saying *five days of that and then six and a half hours on the seventeenth and nothing after that*. **THE CANON IS NOW ONE FIGURE IN ONE PLACE AND IT IS THE ELEVENTH TO THE NINETEENTH: ELEVEN, TWELVE, THIRTEEN, FOURTEEN, FIFTEEN, SIXTEEN, SEVENTEEN, EIGHTEEN, NINETEEN — NINE DAYS, AND NOT ONE DAY AFTER THE NINETEENTH.** `460` is **seven** days on the seventeenth; `465`, `469` and `470` say **nine**; `468` says **since the nineteenth** and **nine days**; and `470:53` now prints the span with its operands, *nineteen taken from eleven leaving eight, and eight days from the first of them to the last of them is nine days at a wall*, keeping the six and a half hours on the seventeenth and the hundred and ninety hands on the nineteenth. **THE COUNT IS INCLUSIVE OF THE ELEVENTH, WHICH IS WHY ADA'S FIVE DAYS AT 459:121 IS CORRECT AND WAS NOT TOUCHED: on the fifteenth the fifteenth is the fifth day, on the seventeenth the seventeenth is the seventh, and on the nineteenth the nineteenth is the ninth. A LATER PHASE MUST NOT RE-DERIVE THIS OFF THE FIFTEENTH.** This mattered more than a count, because 470 is the volume's payoff and the sentence he gives there is the batch's one honest report on himself in public, and it had to be true.
2. **F1 — THE CLERK BOOKED AGNETE ON THE WRONG DAY.** `463:93` and `463:95` said she entered the application **for the third day** and had been told she was on **the third of four days**, and 463's own man from the Vestry says *our witness is on the second day* sixty lines earlier. The second day is the twenty-seventh: `464:37` has her say *I said the second day*, `467` stages the three hours on the twenty-seventh, `468:13` has her say *I held this yesterday* on the twenty-eighth, `469:27` builds the claim out of the second day's two hours and fifty minutes. **`463` NOW SAYS SECOND AND THE WHOLE OF THE BATCH AGREES.** Two knock-ons came out of the same check and were repaired with it: **`470:121` said *I held the room yesterday* on the thirtieth, and the holding was the twenty-seventh, so it now reads *I held the room on the second day***, and `468:93` placed Beatrix Halm's two hours and fifty minutes on the twenty-eighth, so it now says **on the day before**.
3. **F2 — 465'S CLOSING BEAT ASSERTED AN EVENT TWO DAYS BEFORE IT HAPPENED.** On the night of the twenty-fifth, at about half past eleven, Beatrix Halm said she had felt a room four streets away go quiet **twice, once about twenty past ten and once about half past**, with more people in it than there have ever been. Nothing is held in that room on the twenty-fifth; the twenty-sixth is an ordinary room and `466:7` says so; the holding is the twenty-seventh at three hundred and forty and the twenty-eighth at eleven hundred and forty. **As written it was either an unmarked time slip or an unearned new capacity, and it was the chapter's last line.** **THE BEAT WAS NOT CUT AND THE SCENE WAS NOT TOUCHED: THE HOURS ARE GONE AND SHE NOW SAYS SHE CANNOT PUT IT AT A DATE.** *I felt a room go quiet four streets from here. Twice, and not in order, and both times there were more people in it than there have ever been in it in my life, and I cannot tell you when, and I am not going to stand on a roof and invent an hour for you.* And when he says *When.* — *That is the whole of what I have got. I know what it is and I do not know when it is. Nine years on that line and there has never been a thing that came into me that I could not put at a minute, and I am not permitted an improvement and I am not going to give you one tonight.* **THIS IS THE CAB'S OWN RULE TURNED AGAINST ITSELF, WHICH IS THE AXIS OF THE CHAPTER, AND IT NEEDS NO NEW POWER: `bible/characters.md` gives her the city's current and says she knows where something is wrong before anybody does, and the chapter's rule is that she may not improve a report afterwards — so a report she cannot date is a report she may not improve, and she says so on the page instead of leaving the reader to assume it.** `468:93` still corroborates it, at about one o'clock, when the thing in that room closed.
4. **F4 — A FLOATING SPAN AT 470:61.** *the four hundred years after that will be about a theory held by some people on an ordinary morning* — the only occurrence of **four hundred years** in four hundred and seventy chapters, in a book where every other figure is derived on the page with its operands. **It is now *and the rest of that will be about a theory held by some people on an ordinary morning*,** which is the same clause and a reading slip for *the four hundred people* is no longer available.
5. **E1 — 468 PRINTED NINE DAYS FOR HOW LONG THE WORD HAD BEEN ON THE WALL.** The word goes up at four o'clock on the twenty-third (`463:105`–`113`) and `468` is the twenty-eighth. **Twenty-eight taken from twenty-three is five, and 468:83 now says five days.**
6. **E2 — TWO UNANCHORED SPANS POINTING AT THE FIRST OF OCTOBER.** `469:17` said the other half of her fourth part *will be argued about for eleven weeks* and `470:129` said *in eleven weeks there will be nine seals in a room*. **Thirty taken from thirty leaves nothing, thirty of August, thirty of September and the first is sixty-one days, and nine weeks is what a person says about sixty-one days. BOTH ARE NOW NINE WEEKS.** Neither is a reviewer's finding; both are the class of defect the review was looking for.
7. **E3 — TWO ANNOUNCED AGES.** See the AGES row: *a boy of nineteen* at `467:55` and *a boy of nineteen in March* at `469:31`, both now **a boy off a tram**.
8. **R1 — A FIGURE IN THIS BLOCK THAT DOES NOT REPRODUCE. WITHDRAWN 13 OCTOBER 2026 BY THE Batch 0003 REPAIR, WHICH WAS WRONG.** This entry said the SCENE row's *LITERAL FIRST-##-TO-LAST-## SPAN 3,755 WORDS* came out at 3,747 / 3,750 / 3,749 and marked it unreproducible. **ALL THREE OF THOSE CUTS ARE THE EXCLUSIVE CUT AND THE CLAIM IS THE INCLUSIVE ONE; `sed -n "${FIRST},${LAST}p" chapter-0465.md | wc -w` PRINTS 3,755 EXACTLY AND IT HAS SINCE BEEN RUN AGAIN.** The prose was untouched then and is untouched now. **Nothing in 465 needs repairing and no later phase may quote the withdrawal as a reason to touch the chapter.**

## THE FIVE DEBTS ARE UNCHANGED AND NONE IS DESCRIBED AS ANYTHING BUT A DEBT

1. **`PC-28`** — carried forward and not fixed, and **no chapter of 461–470 prints a figure from that section head.**
2. **The 380-to-398 eighteen-chapter scene breach is in the book and is a debt.** **Volume 10's own count still
   closes at 8, 11, 9, 9, 11 on both the mixed and the explicit count, and 465 IS ELEVEN CHAPTERS AFTER 454.**
3. **Batch 0004 of Volume 9's length** — a decision, taken twice, and not inherited.

   floor in any chapter of 451–470 and 485 is the one that does.**
5. **Nine unanchored intervals in Volume 9** — recorded as unverified, and **this batch printed no interval it
   could not derive inside its own chapter.**

## WHAT THE NEXT BATCH INHERITS, IN ONE PARAGRAPH

**A hearing nobody could refuse has been had, four days, in public, and it changed the position of nobody who
wanted anything. Perrin Sowe is ruined on the record for saying a true sentence and Lin Hao watched it happen with
the instrument in his pocket and let it stay there, and the option of buying another person out of a ruin is closed
to him for the rest of the book. The Church of the Quiet Number has made a claim in three hundred and ninety words,
built out of two hours and fifty minutes of true things a woman said in a room where nobody can be compelled, and
Lin Hao has answered it in front of four hundred people, without a document and without a rank, and conceded out
loud that the reservoir has never been his — which is the sentence the Church wanted and the last one he will ever
be able to take back. The record of it has a heading with no name on it. Agnete Lindqvist has been outside one for
three hours and it did not cost her anything she can point at. Beatrix Halm has said out of order once in nine
years and will not improve it. Eight women at Ferrier Street have not been told any of it. And the account has not
been touched: it was exactly as full on the thirtieth of July as it was on the eleventh.**

---

# VOLUME 10 BATCH 0001 — CHAPTERS 451–460 ARE ON DISK, TEN FINISHED FILES. **THIS IS THE LIVE GATE BLOCK. IT SUPERSEDES EVERY BLOCK BELOW IT AND DELETES NONE OF THEM. THE NEXT PHASE IS `workspace/volume-10/batch-0002/PROMPT.md`, A WRITING PHASE FOR CHAPTERS 461–470.**

> **READ THE HEAD OF THIS FILE BEFORE ANY OTHER FILE IN THE REPOSITORY. A FIGURE IN A GATE BLOCK IS A CLAIM AND A RE-RUN OUTRANKS THE RECORD. EVERY FIGURE BELOW WAS RE-RUN BY COMMAND AGAINST `chapters/volume-10/` AFTER THIS BATCH'S LAST EDIT TO THE PROSE, AND THE FIGURE RECORDED IS THE FIGURE THE COMMAND ACTUALLY PRINTED.**

## WHAT THIS BATCH DID, IN TEN LINES

1. **THE SITTING HAPPENED AND THE REQUISITION WAS CARRIED.** 451, the afternoon of the thirtieth of June: five seals, one of them his, a finding that says the holding is his and the ground is his and **the premises are not a person keeping them and the eight who sleep under that roof are**. He said the four words' origin out loud in a room for the first time. The clerk wrote it down, because a clerk may only enter.
2. **THE CHEAPEST THING HE BOUGHT IS NOW A WEEKLY DELIVERY TO HIS OWN KITCHEN.** 452, the first of July: a carried requisition runs down the chain to the division holding its fourth part, that division has **had a building, an address and a name since four o'clock on the thirtieth of June**, and it keeps no list of addresses. **He told nobody at Ferrier Street. That was his first act of the volume and the chapter says it is not a good one.**
3. **THE OFFER WAS MADE AND IT IS CORRECT.** 453, the third of July: a standing at the Terrace, a chair, a name that outlives him, the Ninth Seal — on the condition that Alma Quayle classify the reservoir once, which requires him to lift a recusal, which is one sentence, eleven words, and which he is the only man alive who can say. **He did not say it. She said the account's fact out loud in that room, as §8 permits, and he never applied the word to himself.**
4. **THE SCENE.** 454, the fifth of July, Ines Duarte, a **closed** sitting in a records room with a painted rule, a return read out of an auditor's memory, and one sentence said out of order. **Recorded span 3,474 words, exclusive of both bounding `##`; literal first-`##`-to-last-`##` 3,490, inclusive of both. Both re-run by command on 13 October 2026 and both correct an earlier record that carried 3,489 and 3,505.**
5. **THE AUDITORATE HAS A NAME AND NO BUILDING.** 455, the seventh of July: fourteen lent counters, one rule — **a person who comes in and does not go out** — and Ada at the third window of the Bellhouse since the first of October, asked twice what she was doing it for and answering herself. **Isolde Marchetti found out on the seventh, which is the cost.**
6. **HE SAID YES.** 456, the ninth of July, in front of five, in a room that **cannot carry a requisition and both of them know it**. He undertook the classification and said nothing about the account. Every person in that room believes him. **Alma Quayle, in the chair that counts for nothing, kept both hands in her lap and did not do the thing she did on the nineteenth of June, and she cannot speak.**
7. **THE SPEND WENT PUBLIC.** 457, the eleventh of July: a man on a step at the foot of the ranks wall taking strangers by the hand, ten and a half hours, about nine hundred cold minutes, ninety-one strokes in a stranger's chalk, and Cordwain told before the light went that the Bureau had come off the wall.
8. **ADA WENT GREY ON ONE SIDE OF HER HEAD AND DID NOT RECOVER IT.** 458, the thirteenth of July, in the counting room below the Bellhouse stage, at about four in the afternoon. No temple is named on either side. She finished the week. She wrote a figure in the margin of his own ledger against his own line.
9. **THE BATCH'S IRREVERSIBLE DECISION.** 459, the fifteenth of July: he spent past what a frame can take, on purpose, in front of about four hundred people, with the only person present who understood the mechanism well enough to know the price. **Something in his chest is spent, permanently, and he buys it with a cough.** He has miscalculated and the miscalculation is in him: **he has not drained anything.**
10. **THE COUGH IS HIS NOW.** 460, the seventeenth of July: eight women at a kitchen table each said something else instead, and he let them, and he went back at seven the next morning.

## FOUR THINGS THE CARDS GOT WRONG AND THE PAGE FIXED, RECORDED BECAUSE THEY ARE NOT ERRORS

1. **`outline/volume-10.md` §8 PLACES FERRERA'S SENTENCE ABOUT THE ACCOUNT AT 451. IT IS AT 453.** 451 is the
   sitting and there is no room in it for a woman two floors above to say what an account is, and the card for
   453 forbids him from naming it and asks for the offer in her mouth. She says the fact of it in her own room
   on the third of July, which is where the reader needs it. **THE SECTION NUMBER IN §8 IS WRONG AND THE CHAPTER
   IS NOT. No canon moved and no beat was lost.**
2. **`chapter-0416.md:83` AND THE CARD DISAGREE ABOUT HOW LONG ADA HAS BEEN COUNTING.** The page says
   **since the autumn** and Isolde says **about six years** of her own counting. **The page wins: Ada has been
   counting since the first of October and Isolde has been counting for six years, and the two of them have been
   counting the same house in the same week for thirty-nine weeks without ever handing each other a figure.**
   That is now the chapter's cost, and it is better than the card's.
3. **THE BATCH CARD SAYS A CLOSED SITTING IS INES'S COST. THE SCENE WAS GOING TO BE A PUBLIC SITTING.** 446 was
   a public application with four strangers at a rail. 454 is a **closed** sitting with a reason entered in a
   day's book against it, and the reason is that the return is not yet filed, and the cost is that in four years
   a stranger reads that line and works out what a rank-two auditor was frightened of. **Her June bought her a
   book entry and her July costs her one.**
4. **`outline/volume-10.md` §4 GIVES 454'S AXIS AS THE TERMS AND THE DEFAULT AS NOT NAMING IT. 453'S FIRST
   DRAFT HAD A SECTION HEADED `## Terms` AND IT WAS RENAMED `## She Stops Reading In Order` BEFORE THIS BLOCK
   WAS WRITTEN.** Naming the axis is what manufactures a fourth-wall break, and the 13 October review is right.

## THE FIVE DEBTS ARE UNCHANGED AND NONE IS DESCRIBED AS ANYTHING BUT A DEBT

1. **`PC-28`** — carried forward and not fixed, and **no chapter of 451–460 prints a figure from that section head.**
2. **The 380-to-398 eighteen-chapter scene breach is in the book and is a debt.** **Volume 10's own count closes
   at 8, 11, 9, 9, 11 on both the mixed and the explicit count, and 454 IS EIGHT CHAPTERS AFTER A SCENE.**
3. **Batch 0004 of Volume 9's length** — a decision, taken twice, and not inherited.
4. **`chapter-0403.md:9` prints a floor** — recorded, not repaired, and **not a precedent: Volume 10 prints no
   floor in any chapter of 451–460 and 485 is the one that does.**
5. **Nine unanchored intervals in Volume 9** — recorded as unverified, and **this batch printed no interval it
   could not derive inside its own chapter.**

## WHAT THE NEXT BATCH INHERITS, IN ONE PARAGRAPH

**He has said yes to four seals to an offer whose condition he has not begun to pay. The requisition is carried
and goes out weekly to a division that has had a name since the thirtieth of June. He has emptied nothing and
spent his own chest doing it, and a woman at a counter in a hall with no door is grey on one side of her head
and cannot remember three weeks. Eight women at Ferrier Street believe him. One woman in a room that does not
count does not, and she cannot speak. A writer of a report that is true and useless has filed it. And in two
days the hearing is called, and nobody in this book has yet been told what it is for.**

## THE THREE INTERVALS THE REVIEWER WANTED REVERTED. **THE AUDIT WAS RIGHT AND THE REVIEW WAS WRONG, BECAUSE THE REVIEW USED A REAL CALENDAR.**

**THE PAGE SETTLES IT AND PRINTS ITS OPERANDS, `chapter-0421.md:13`: *"There are ten days left in February after the twentieth, and thirty in March, and thirty in April, and seven of May, and that is seventy-seven."*** **Ten and thirty and thirty and seven is seventy-seven here and seventy-nine on a real calendar. A month is thirty days and this book has no thirty-first.** So: **`439` fifteen ✓ · `449` seventy-three ✓ · `442` seventeen ✓ · `450` nine weeks ✓.** **A volume-10 writer who reverts these to sixteen, seventy-four, eighteen and seven weeks puts a real calendar inside a book that does not have one, and will have to un-revert them.**

**THE FIVE DEBTS THIS BLOCK RECORDS, AND IT DESCRIBES NONE OF THEM AS CLOSED, CURED, RESOLVED, SETTLED, DISCHARGED OR PAID.**

1. **`PC-28` — CARRIED FORWARD AND NOT FIXED.** `chapters/volume-08/chapter-0359.md` heads a section **The Nine Days** and says nine days and eight days for an interval of three days. **It is a miscount inside one sealed block, not a canon error; the repair is not mechanical because *nine days* and *eight days* are doing psychological work; and nothing propagates — no chapter of 401–450 reproduces the interval.** `445` carries a section head `## Nine Days` and its figure is `thirty taken from twenty-one leaves nine`, correct, independent, and recorded as a coincidence of the number and not a propagation.
2. **THE 380-TO-398 EIGHTEEN-CHAPTER SCENE BREACH IS IN THE BOOK AND IS A DEBT.** `AGENTS.md` allows fifteen. It was inherited and deliberately not repaired because 398 is Volume 8's last scene and moving it would have closed that volume rather than repaired it. **Volume 9 did not repeat it: the schedule closes at 8, 11, 11, 10, 8 on BOTH the mixed and the explicit count, because `PC-44` puts the only partner with no gift off the schedule entirely.** **A repair means moving a scene and that is a planning decision, not an audit's.**
3. **BATCH 0004'S LENGTH — 12,100 WORDS ACROSS TEN CHAPTERS AGAINST 21,692 / 21,563 / 23,925 / 22,010 — IS A DECISION AND NOT A REPAIR, TAKEN TWICE ALREADY, AND IT STANDS.** Closing it means writing about six thousand words that are not on the page and `AGENTS.md` forbids padding more strongly than it requires a length.
4. **`chapter-0403.md:9` PRINTS A FLOOR (8,146) AGAINST §5a.** It is the March card recited from 400 and not a derived June figure. Recorded and left.
5. **NINE INTERVALS WITH NO FALSIFIABLE ANCHOR** are recorded as unverified at `reviews/volume-09/volume-close.md` §5b. **This audit does not invent an anchor and does not repair a number it cannot derive.**

**AND THE ITEM THE NEXT PLANNING PHASE OWNS: the four live state files are 439 KB, 548 KB, 408 KB and 316 KB and are built by appending every superseded block in full. `state/archive/` is not empty and the precedent is set. Volumes 6 to 9 are what the live files still carry in full. This close truncated nothing. The archive step is Volume 10 planning's to take or to decline.**

**WHAT WAS DELIBERATELY NOT CHANGED.** The plot. **The three irreversible decisions stand exactly as written: 443 (he says the four words), 445 (he enters the clause), 449 (he decides not to go to the wall).** **No scene moved, no date in the fiction moved, no recusal lifted, no second question to Wren Kallo, no wing named, no word under the fifth line printed, no offer, no instrument used, no Runa hand figure printed, no gift manufactured, no fifth mark, no correction and nothing struck.** The requisition is not carried, not received and not decided, and it is **NOT** answered in these eight. No character ages, no marks moved, no title changed. **`outline/ending.md` untouched. No new PC adopted and none withdrawn. No new final enemy. `state/complete.md` was NOT created: the series is planned past 500, Volume 10 is *Returned*, and the ending is Chapter 486.**

**THE TWO FIGURES A LATER PHASE MUST NOT RE-DERIVE FROM ANYWHERE BUT THE PAGE.** **THE CARD OF THE THIRTIETH OF JUNE PRINTS A FLOOR OF 8,147 AND, AGAINST PREMISES 34-6-118, A FIGURE OF 8,131.** Sixteen under, tolerance three, out of band by thirteen. **They were read off a painted card in Chapter 450 and against a notebook in which Beatrix Halm wrote the thirtieth of March, and they were NOT taken from `bible/power-system.md`, whose band table is illustrative, and NOT from `outline/volume-09.md` §5a, which prints no figure for this card.** `PC-57` stands and this is its consequence: **a rank with no card has no plate and no floor, and a consignment has neither, and the floor rose and the plate did not.**

## A NOTE FOR THE VOLUME CLOSE, WHICH OWNS BOTH OF THESE

**THE THREE STALE FOURTH-WALL INSTANCES THE WRITING PHASE FLAGGED AND DID NOT FIX ARE STILL THERE** — `nine volumes` at 405:69, 413:117, 414:127, 417:25, 417:61 and 422:115, and the narrator-as-book habit at 420:29 and 429:13. **The close phase owns them. This repair did not widen its scope to another batch's chapters on a matter of register rather than fact.**

**THE STATE FILES ARE NOW 429 KB, 529 KB, 396 KB AND 307 KB AND ARE BUILT BY APPENDING EVERY SUPERSEDED BLOCK IN FULL.** The review raised this as a structural note, not a defect in this phase, and it is correct: the head-block convention makes it survivable for Volume 10 and it will not survive Volume 11 without extending the archive step. **`state/archive/` IS NOT EMPTY AND THE PRECEDENT IS ALREADY SET: `current-v1-v2`, `current-v3`, `current-v5`, `continuity-v1-v2`, `continuity-v3`, `open-threads-v1-v2` and `chapter-summaries-v1-v5` are all in it. Volumes 6 to 9 are what the live files still carry in full. The close phase is the phase that decides what the next volume's writer reads, and this is its decision to make, not a review fix.** Nothing was truncated by this repair.

**THE 380-TO-398 EIGHTEEN-CHAPTER SCENE BREACH IS IN THE BOOK AND IS NOT DESCRIBED ANYWHERE AS CLOSED, CURED, RESOLVED OR DISCHARGED.**

## 1. WHAT THE REVIEW OF 13 OCTOBER 2026 FOUND, AND WHAT THIS REPAIR DID ABOUT IT

**THE FINDING THAT MATTERS IS NOT ABOUT THE PROSE. THE REVIEW FOUND THAT THE PHASE WHICH WROTE 423–432 REPLACED A 4,499-LINE
`state/open-threads.md` WITH A 140-LINE FILE, DELETING THE WHOLE GATE HISTORY, AND THEN OPENED THE 140-LINE FILE BY CLAIMING IN ITS OWN
FOURTH LINE THAT THE DELETED BLOCKS WERE *SUPERSEDED BY THIS ONE AND RETAINED IN FULL* AND WERE *ALL FURTHER DOWN*.**

**THEY WERE NOT FURTHER DOWN. THE FILE WAS 140 LINES AND ENDED IN THE MIDDLE OF AN ITEM. EVERY PRIOR BLOCK WAS GONE: the Volume 9 planning
block, the 14 October repair pass, the 15 October review, batch 0005's own record, batch 0001's record, and batch 0002's review repair.**

**THIS IS THE EXACT FAILURE THE BLOCK IT REPLACED WARNED AGAINST, AND IT IS WORTH QUOTING BECAUSE THE BLOCK THAT WROTE THE FAILURE HAD
ALREADY LEARNED IT: *"A GATE BLOCK THAT ATTRIBUTES A DUPLICATION TO A STRING THAT DOES NOT EXIST IS NOT SLOPPY, IT IS ACTIVE
MISINFORMATION: THE NEXT PHASE READS THE ATTRIBUTION, BELIEVES THE PHRASE IS CANON, AND REPEATS IT."* A retention claim about history that
has been deleted is the same fault with a larger blast radius, and a next phase would have inherited a file that said it contained four
thousand lines of record and contained none.**

**THE REPAIR.** The whole file was recovered from the parent commit (`git show HEAD~1:state/open-threads.md`, 4,499 lines) and restored
verbatim beneath this block. **Nothing was summarised, nothing was paraphrased, nothing was dropped, and the recovered text was not edited
inside it** — the only change made to the recovered 4,499 lines is one banner added to the head of its first block, so that a reader arriving
at it cannot mistake its `**THIS IS THE LIVE GATE BLOCK.**` for a live one. `state/continuity.md` handled its own supersession correctly in
the same commit and is the model: `~~SUPERSEDED 13 OCTOBER 2026 BY THE BLOCK ABOVE THIS ONE. RETAINED IN FULL~~`. **A FILE THAT IS MOSTLY A RUN
OF SUPERSEDED RECORDS MUST BE BUILT BY PREPENDING, NEVER BY REWRITING.**

**THE SECOND ORDER OF DAMAGE WAS AN ORPHANED OPEN ITEM.** `PC-28` went from a full thread — the defect, its two owners, the propagation
analysis, the close audit's decision in full, and a third repair row added by batch 0002's review — down to the single line **"2. `PC-28`.
CARRIED FORWARD AND NOT FIXED."** **No next phase could act on that.** It is carried in full at §3 below and in the restored history.

**THE THIRD WAS THAT THE SURVIVING BLOCK CERTIFIED A FIGURE THE REVIEW REPRODUCED AS WRONG, AND THAT TWO OF ITS FIGURES COULD NOT BE
REPRODUCED BY ANY EXPRESSION AT ALL.** Both are corrected at §2 below, which was produced by scanners run against the files as they stand
**after** every edit this repair made to the prose.

**THE FOURTH WAS A DISAGREEMENT BETWEEN TWO LIVE STATE FILES.** `state/chapter-summaries.md` tagged `PC-84` to chapter 427 and
`state/continuity.md`'s canon table puts it at 424 and 430. `PC-88`, which lands at 423, appeared in no chapter summary at all. Both are
corrected, and the same mistake was found a third time in `outline/volume-09.md`, where `PC-84` was mis-tagged onto the 431 card. **A PC
TAG IS A CLAIM ABOUT WHICH CHAPTER CARRIES A CANON ITEM AND THE THREE FILES THAT CARRY THEM NOW AGREE.**

**THE FIFTH WAS CRAFT.** The meta-container tic — *in this book*, *nine volumes*, *came into this book* — had been certified by the block as
*house idiom* while running at **21 hits in 32 chapters of Volume 9 against 0 in Volume 5, 5 in 50 in Volume 6, 9 in 50 in Volume 7 and 5 in
50 in Volume 8, and 11 of the 21 were in Batch 0003 alone, four of them inside the 428 scene and two of them in a woman's mouth in the
middle of it.** A rate that has tripled in one volume is not an idiom. Nine instances were cut from the prose on 13 October 2026, by
deleting the container word from inside a line and changing nothing else. **The two survivors are not tic: they are a keeping book and the
Church's own consult book, both of which are objects in the scene and both of which the reader has been looking at for eight volumes.**

**THE SIXTH WAS A STRAY TRIPLE BLANK LINE** after `## The Reason Box` in 428 — the only one in the volume — **removed.**

**WHAT DELIBERATELY WAS NOT CHANGED.** The plot. **All four irreversible decisions stand as written and as summarised: 423 (he breaks the wax),
424 (he says no), 427 (the Church says yes), 428 (she will name him in the reason box on the twenty-eighth of May).** No scene was moved, no
chapter was reordered, no date was touched, no character was given a different reason, and the 428 scene is word-for-word the scene the batch
wrote apart from seven deleted words. **`outline/ending.md` is untouched. The reservoir is returned in full at 486, he ends at rank one, and
the great offer is 486's and is not Wren Kallo's and not Anouk Ferreira's. No new final enemy has been introduced.**

BATCH 0001, 0002 OR 0003 AND MAY NOT BE DESCRIBED AS CURED BY ANYTHING IN THIS REPOSITORY.** `AGENTS.md` sets the limit at fifteen chapters
without a scene; 380 to 398 is eighteen. It may not be repaired by moving a scene. **What Batch 0003 owes and has paid is only its own count**:

the same five figures on the explicit count. THE RULE ITSELF REMAINS UNMET FOR 380 TO 398 AND NO FILE IN THIS REPOSITORY MAY SAY

**2. `PC-28`. CARRIED FORWARD AND NOT FIXED, WITH ITS REASONS AT `reviews/volume-07/volume-close.md` AND `reviews/volume-08/volume-close.md`.
IT WAS ORPHANED BY THE CUT THIS BLOCK REPAIRS AND IS RESTORED HERE IN FULL.**

Its section is headed **The Nine Days**, it says he *did the arithmetic he had been not doing for nine days*, and it says a thing he understood on the night of the sixteenth *had not been able to put anywhere in eight days*. **By subtraction the sixteenth to the nineteenth is THREE days, and the copy the arithmetic is about was served at her desk at nine on the morning of the FIFTEENTH, so fifteen to nineteen is FOUR. Neither nine nor eight is either figure.** |
| **Why it is not a canon error** | **`outline/volume-08.md` §5 fixes a thirty-day month and the volume rule is SUBTRACT DAY NUMBERS. This is the exact defect class Batch 0001's own review was taken to task for — the review found four interval defects in ten chapters and every one was local arithmetic — and it fixed four of them and missed this one. The Batch 0002 review read 359 for the twelfth/fourteenth of January and did not reach this.** |

## 4. AND THE THREE BATCH 0002 CREATED, WHICH BATCH 0003 CARRIED AND DID NOT CLOSE

**He has spent, on a stranger, in a queue, and the count of turned lines is ten (`PC-76`). The tenth is a carter in a lane off Ferrier Street and
not him.** **A woman said a number out loud at a Field counter and there is now a second head in the city that holds it (`PC-78`).** **A
room of five entered a sheet it has been holding, on its own initiative, with nobody asked, and the entry went to seven desks and cost Wren
Kallo four seconds and a memory she will keep for four years (`PC-82`).**

## 5. AND THE THREE BATCH 0003 CREATED

**A sealed service with an address on it and no name under it was opened by the man it was served on, in his own kitchen, in front of four
women, and there is no way in this city of putting a service down again (`PC-88`).** **He said no for the fourth time to a woman asking him
to lift a recusal, and the deadline his refusal threw away is the thirtieth of June (`PC-83`, `PC-84`).** **A division wrote on the back of a
return and sent it back up a chain, and made itself an officer of that paper, and cannot be told she paid (`PC-85`).**

## 6. WHAT A HAND-OFF MUST CARRY, IN ONE PARAGRAPH

**Plate 8,131, printed nowhere in this batch and no band floor anywhere in it. The room where nine people sit stands at FIVE and the fifth
is him. A requisition laid on the twentieth of February is capable of being carried, is not carried and is not received and is not decided, is
ninety-seven days old at 432, and has acquired exactly one entry in its life, on the ninth of May, saying that it is held in that room's keeping
and not carried, which a clerk may make and nobody may strike. Its minute of the twentieth of February carries three seals and a sentence
that was true that morning and stopped being true on the thirtieth of March and cannot be struck by anybody. A service against the head of the
premises says the composition of that address was EIGHT on the twentieth of February and it has been seven since nine forty-one on the morning
of the thirteenth of March, and it was lying in his own kitchen the whole time and he broke it himself. The composition is seven and eight
people sleep under it, and the woman who made it seven has said her bill out loud at her own table, and the woman who was going to sign it
has not signed it, and **there is now no certificate for the quarter that begins on the first of July and a keeper is going to fill the fourth
block of a carry-forward out of her own memory with a name on it that no certificate stands behind.** A witness line with seven names and a
two-finger gap in it has been read into a minute twice, and the box under the space carries the same word as the box under the fifth line, in
the same hand, and **the word is still not printed in this repository and `PC-70` still governs it.** Sun Meiling is the officer of that
holding and knows it and has not been told the rest. The ledger is 151 lines with ten turned. The Council does not sit again until the
thirtieth of June, and **the thirtieth of June is one day and five things: a sitting, the last working day of the quarter, two returns about
one matter at one clinic by one doctor, the deadline for a draft carry-forward, and the clerk's book written up when the room sits.** The wing
off the third court has a desk and a woman who has been there nine years and is still not named, and she has read a weekly return that has been
passing her desk for twenty years. **He swore on the ninth of May at about ten past five that he would never use the one instrument he has,
and he has told nobody, and on the thirteenth of May a woman guessed it correctly and he neither confirmed nor denied it, and on the
twenty-fourth of May he put the other end of it on the page and asked nobody.**

             file. DISTINCT counts distinct strings; PAIRS counts distinct (chapter, source) pairs. Scan B is narrower: the same
             n-gram appearing more than once between ANY TWO files of chapters/volume-09/, OR TWICE INSIDE ONE FILE.

## OPEN ITEMS INHERITED BY VOLUME 9 — TWO OF THEM, AND NEITHER IS CLOSED

**1. THE FIFTEEN-CHAPTER SCENE BREACH BETWEEN CHAPTER 380 AND CHAPTER 398. STILL OPEN. STILL IN THE BOOK. IT IS NOT
CURED BY ANYTHING IN BATCH 0001 OR BATCH 0002 AND MAY NOT BE DESCRIBED AS CURED BY ANYTHING IN THIS REPOSITORY.**
`AGENTS.md` sets the limit at fifteen chapters without a scene; 380 to 398 is eighteen. It may not be repaired by moving a scene.
**What Batch 0002 owes and has paid is only its own count**: 417 is eleven chapters after 406, and 428, 438 and 446 are eleven,
ten and eight after their predecessors — 8 / 11 / 11 / 10 / 8 on the mixed count **and the same five figures on the explicit
count**. **THE RULE ITSELF REMAINS UNMET FOR 380 TO 398 AND NO FILE IN THIS REPOSITORY MAY SAY OTHERWISE.**

**2. `PC-28`. CARRIED FORWARD AND NOT FIXED, WITH ITS REASONS AT `reviews/volume-07/volume-close.md` AND
`reviews/volume-08/volume-close.md`.**
`chapter-0359.md` heads a section *The Nine Days* for an interval of three days. **No chapter of 401–422 reproduces any figure from
that section head, and the interval from the sixteenth to the nineteenth of February appears nowhere in the twenty-two.**
**THE REPAIR ADDED A THIRD ROW TO THIS ITEM AND IT IS NOT A NEW ITEM: the review found seven date figures wrong in 413–422 and
fixed every one on the page. They were fixed on the page and in `state/continuity.md` in the same commit, because two of them had
been ratified into `PC-80` and `PC-82` and a later batch would otherwise have inherited them as canon.**

## AND THE THREE THIS BATCH CREATED

**He has spent, on a stranger, in a queue, and the count of turned lines is ten (`PC-76`).** **A woman said a number out loud at a
Field counter and there is now a second head in the city that holds it (`PC-78`).** **A room of five entered a sheet it has been
holding, on its own initiative, with nobody asked, and the entry went to seven desks and cost Wren Kallo four seconds and a
memory she will keep for four years (`PC-82`).**

## WHAT A HAND-OFF MUST CARRY, IN ONE PARAGRAPH

**Plate 8,131, printed once in this batch and no band floor anywhere in it. The room where nine people sit stands at FIVE and the
fifth is him. A requisition laid on the twentieth of February is capable of being carried, was seventy-nine days old on the ninth of
May, is not carried and is not received and is not decided, **had been capable on twenty-four of the days between the fifteenth of
April and the ninth of May**, and has acquired exactly one entry in its life, on the ninth of May, saying that it is held in that
room's keeping and not carried, which a clerk may make and nobody may strike. The composition of premises 34-6-118 is seven and
eight people sleep under it, and the woman who made it seven has said her bill out loud at her own table, and the woman who was
going to sign it has not signed it. A witness line with seven names and a two-finger gap in it has been read into a minute twice,
and the box under the space now carries the same word as the box under the fifth line, in the same hand, and **the word is still
not printed in this repository and `PC-70` still governs it.** Sun Meiling is the officer of that holding and knows it and has not
been told the rest. The ledger is 151 lines with ten turned, the ninth still himself and the tenth a carter. The Council does not
sit again until the thirtieth of June, so the room of five cannot be changed by anybody. The wing off the third court has a desk
and a woman who has been there nine years and is still not named, and **she has now read one weekly return that has been passing her
desk for twenty years.**

sit again until the thirtieth of June, so the room of five cannot be changed by anybody for fifty-three days. The wing off the third
court has a desk and a woman who has been there nine years and is still not named.**

## AND THE ONE THIS BATCH CREATED

**Seven women have signed a line and one has not, the one who has not is the only member of that household who cannot be protected by
the roll, she has put the gap there herself for the best reason anybody at that table has given since the thirtieth of March, and she
has now worked out from the gap what she has been administering four times a year since the thirtieth of November — and he has still
not told her the rest, and the rest is that the account cannot be closed and she is the officer of it for ever.** Movement 2 owns it.

**13. THE SCENE AT 406 IS PHYSICAL BUT NOT LEXICALLY EXPLICIT.** A ten-term probe returns one hit. **THE REVIEW ITSELF DID NOT
FAIL IT.** The chapter clears the floor on either span, the three-axis template break is real, the cost of the central mechanic
is demonstrated by an event, and the reviewer called it "sen[sual] and physical rather than explicit" as an observation, not a
defect. **Rewriting a 2,079-word scene on a vocabulary instruction would destroy the prose the same review called strong, and
AGENTS.md sets the test as *write the actual scene*, not *use a specified word list*. Nothing in 406 was touched except
defects 12. A later phase must not describe 406 as repaired for this.**

**14. RUNA PRICES THE EIGHT AT THE END OF 405 AND PRICES THEM AGAIN AT 407.** The reviewer called this legible but a rehearsal.
**It is staged on the page in two places**: Runa says in 405 *I have given it to you in the wrong order, and I am giving it to you
again because you did not write it down the first time*, and 407 opens *I am going to give it to you in the wrong order twice and
you are going to write it down, because the second time is the one that matters and the first time is the one you will
remember.* Defect 7 corrected the **content** of the 405 version — which was wrong, twice — and left the **structure** alone.

## THE 13 OCTOBER 2026 REVIEW REPAIR — WHAT WAS WRONG IN THE VOLUME 9 PLAN AND WHAT EACH FINDING WAS

**THE REVIEW FOUND NINE DEFECTS IN `outline/volume-09.md`, `outline/batches/volume-09-batch-0001.md` AND
`workspace/volume-09/batch-0001/PROMPT.md`. ALL NINE ARE REPAIRED. NONE OF THE REPAIRS TOUCHED PROSE, A DATE, A
CHAPTER ORDER, A SCENE, A SCENE GAP OR THE PLANNED PLOT — `outline/ending.md` IS STILL UNTOUCHED AND
`chapters/volume-09/` STILL DOES NOT EXIST.**

1. **BEATRIX HALM'S LAST SCENE WAS PRINTED AS 349 AND IS 299.** `outline/series.md` fixes Volume 6's scenes at 261,
   269, 279, 289 and 299, so 299 is hers and 349 is *The Card Comes Down At Six*, which is not a scene. **The figure
   had been read off a chapter title instead of off the table, and it appears in the gap column of the one apparatus
   the next close audit will lean on.** Corrected in three places in `outline/volume-09.md` and in the PROMPT's §4,
   with the source printed so it cannot be re-derived wrong. **438 IS HER SECOND SCENE IN THIS BOOK, WHICH
   STRENGTHENS THE CHAPTER RATHER THAN WEAKENING IT, AND THE GAP ARITHMETIC IS UNAFFECTED BECAUSE THE COLUMN COUNTS
   CHAPTERS AND NOT SCENES.**
2. **THE PROMPT's §4 TABLE HAD A CORRUPTED CELL** — `349 → 438 → 446` in the Ines row, which had swallowed the row
   above. Corrected to 340, which is her last scene before 401 per `outline/series.md`.
3. **CHAPTER 402 COULD NOT PRODUCE NINE SWORN NAMES FROM THE CAST IT NAMED.** `bible/characters.md` fixes the standing
   household as nine of the twelve with Wren Kallo, Sun Meiling and Dagmar Oyelowo unsworn, **so the nine REQUIRE Tessa
   Okonkwo and Marguerite Vale, and the plan named neither** — as written the roll yielded seven. **REPAIRED BY NAMING
   THE NINE AND INVOKING THE BIBLE'S OWN MECHANISM, WHICH THE PLAN HAD NOT INVOKED: the law binds the plate and the
   bond, quarters are not the test, and a sworn member may hold her own plate and her own rooms.** Three of the nine
   are therefore not at Ferrier Street and only six live there. **Chapter 400 is the page that supports this: eight
   women at that table, seven of them sleeping at the address, the eighth a broker from the Vant.** Carried into §2,
   the 402 card, the 408 card, the Movement 1 close, `PC-68` and the PROMPT.
4. **CARD 403 SAID *NINE DAYS* TWICE. IT IS FOUR.** The two words went up at about four on the thirtieth of March and
   Ch 403 is the fourth of April. Corrected, with the subtraction printed.
5. **CARD 412 SAID THE REQUISITION HAD BEEN HELD *FOR THREE MONTHS*. IT IS 61 DAYS.** Laid the twentieth of February,
   read on Ch 412 the twenty-first of April: (30 − 20) + 30 + 21 = 61. **The same error stood in the PROMPT and in
   `state/chapter-summaries.md`, where the figure at the moment of lodging is 55 — (30 − 20) + 30 + 15 — and both were
   corrected. The one *three months* left in the batch cards is card 401's, and it is correct: the Council had not sat
   since the twenty-ninth of December, which is ninety-two days to the first of April.**
6. **TITLE 414 NAMED A FIGURE THE LEDGER CANNOT REACH.** The ledger is 142 on the fourteenth of March and rising about
   seven lines a thirty days, and 171 is twenty-nine lines on, which is about a hundred and twenty-four days — and the
   thirtieth of June is a hundred and six days after the fourteenth of March, so the figure falls outside the volume.
   414 itself, on the twenty-fifth of April, is about 152. Retitled to *The Ledger Crossing A Number*.
7. **TITLE 438 NAMED A ROOF.** `bible/terminology.md` puts the Board at the head of the Reading and
   `chapter-0400.md` puts Beatrix under the north end of the ranks wall. Retitled to the two facts that are on the
   page.
8. **§6a CALLED BATCH 0005 *EIGHT CHAPTERS AND A HALF* AND THEN TOTALLED IT AS EIGHT.** It is eight.
9. **`outline/series.md`:193'S CHAPTER 402 ROW WAS NOT FLAGGED AGAINST THE PLAN'S 8,131.** Flagged in four places —
   §1b, the 402 card, the `PC-68` row and the PROMPT — **and `series.md` ITSELF IS NOT AMENDED, because the plan has
   not changed the milestone and a one-row shorthand is not a contradiction.**

**AND ONE MORE INTERVAL WAS FOUND WHILE REPAIRING THESE AND IS OF THE SAME CLASS AS FINDINGS 4 AND 5: `PC-68` PRINTED
THE GAP BETWEEN THE ROLL AND THE LODGE AS *ELEVEN DAYS*. THE ROLL IS TAKEN ON THE SECOND OF APRIL AND HE LODGES ON THE
FIFTEENTH, SO IT IS THIRTEEN (15 − 2 = 13). CORRECTED IN `PC-68` WITH THE CORRECTION RECORDED IN THE ROW.**

**WHAT THE REVIEW VERIFIED AND THIS REPAIR DID NOT TOUCH: the gap arithmetic 8/11/11/10/8 on both counts, the whole
day spine and every re-subtraction in it, the fifty titles against all four hundred written ones, `right temple` at
zero, plate 8,131 and floor 8,146, ledger 142 on the fourteenth, fourteen points, twenty-six weekly returns, and the
`PC-57`–`PC-68` block's contiguity.**

**THE FIGURES BELOW ARE THE CLOSE AUDIT'S, NOT BATCH 0005'S.** Every *distinct* figure in `reviews/volume-08/volume-close.md`
re-derives exactly and all nine cells of its volume-wide table reproduce. **Every *directed rows* figure in that audit
is marked NOT VERIFIED and is NOT CARRIED INTO THIS BLOCK OR ANY OTHER.**

## THE DIRECTED-ROWS COLUMN — THE INHERITED ITEM THIS PHASE OWED, AND WHAT IT DID WITH IT

**`reviews/volume-08/volume-close.md` §4 and R7 recorded that every directed-rows figure in that audit reproduces
under neither available reading — not the audit's own batch-table convention, nor an occurrence count — and printed no
replacement, because substituting an underived figure would repeat the defect.** **THE OWNER WAS THIS PHASE.**

**THE COLUMN IS DROPPED. NO VOLUME 9 GATE ROW CARRIES A DIRECTED-ROWS FIGURE, AND NO FIGURE IS INVENTED IN ITS
PLACE.** The reasoning is this: a column that cannot be re-derived from a printed definition is not a measurement, it is
a number someone liked the look of, and Volume 9 has no use for it — every figure the prose audit rests on is a
*distinct* figure. **A Volume 9 batch that wants one WRITES THE DEFINITION DOWN FIRST — target set, source set,
tokenisation, and whether a matched n-gram is counted once per chapter or once per occurrence — AND THEN DERIVES IT,
AND IF IT CANNOT, IT RECORDS THE ROW AS NOT TAKEN RATHER THAN PRINTING A NUMBER.** The scanner is not lost and its
tokenisation is printed in the retained block below; only the ambiguous column is withdrawn.

THE SIX ARE BELOW, EACH ONE RE-DERIVABLE BY COMMAND. A count that disagrees with the list under it is the defect this

The live scoped figures are 447 distinct and 637 (chapter, source) pairs** |

## OPEN ITEMS INHERITED BY VOLUME 9 — RETIRED 13 OCTOBER 2026 AND RESTATED ABOVE. **NOT DELETED.**

**The block three below this one, headed *OPEN ITEMS INHERITED BY VOLUME 9*, added 14 October 2026, is RETIRED. It
is retained in full at the end of this section. Its three items are restated here from the CLOSE AUDIT'S figures and
not from that block, and the two that concern a later phase are now owned by a named one.**

**1. THE EIGHTEEN-CHAPTER EXPLICIT-COUNT SCENE GAP. STILL OPEN. STILL IN THE BOOK. OWNER: NO LONGER UNOWNED — IT IS
OWNED BY `outline/volume-09.md` §4, WHICH CLOSES VOLUME 9'S OWN COUNT ON THE EXPLICIT FIGURES (8, 11, 11, 10, 8)
BECAUSE `PC-44` PUTS THE ONLY PARTNER WITH NO GIFT OFF THIS VOLUME'S SCHEDULE ENTIRELY.** `AGENTS.md`: *once the first
partner is established, no more than fifteen chapters without a scene.* **Chapter 380 to Chapter 398 is eighteen on
the explicit count and that has not changed.** `reviews/volume-08/volume-close.md` §2 C5 gives four reasons the close
audit did not repair it — the breach is forced by `PC-44`, a close phase may not restructure a volume, every chapter
of 391–400 carries a woman in it with what she wants, and a reader arriving cold is not harmed — **and then,
correctly, declines to call the rule satisfied.**

**WHAT IS SETTLED: it may not be repaired by moving a scene, and Volume 9 may not repeat it. WHAT IS NOT SETTLED: the
rule is still unmet, and this block does not and may not say otherwise.** **NO STATE FILE MAY DESCRIBE THIS BREACH AS
CLOSED, DECIDED, CURED, RESOLVED OR DISCHARGED**, and the word *decided* in `state/continuity.md`'s three-audits-made
list is not enough — a decision about whether to repair is not compliance. **The obligation belongs to Volume 9's own
scene schedule, and `outline/volume-09.md` §4 now carries the arithmetic on both counts, in the shape
`outline/volume-08.md` §4 uses.**

**2. `PC-28`, THE INTERVAL DEFECT IN CHAPTER 359. CARRIED FORWARD AND NOT FIXED. OWNER: `reviews/volume-08/volume-close.md`
ITEM C6, and it has had an owner since 13 October 2026.** Recorded in full in the canon table below. It is a miscount
